  - bounded by caps that platform.toml declares, in an optional `[sandbox.served_bytes]` table: `max_files` files (1000 by default) and `max_directories` directories (1000), the first the log shows, and `max_ranges_per_file` ranges a file (64), the lowest. Each cap must be an integer of at least 1, and anything else, or an unknown key, is refused at load with an error naming the file and the key. The snapshot records the caps in effect as `caps` (`{"max_files", "max_directories", "max_ranges_per_file"}`), in every snapshot, a mount's and an unknown one's included. A declared table is in the campaign fingerprint (`PlatformConfig.fingerprint()`, under `served_bytes`), since the caps decide what a run's evidence can show: a resume under other caps is refused, naming them (`platform.served_bytes.max_files: recorded 2000, now 4000`). Without the table the fingerprint keeps the shape it had before the caps could be declared, so a campaign that ran under the defaults still resumes; declaring the table, even with the default values, is then a change the resume refuses. `files_total` and `listings_total` count every file and directory served, listed or not; `files_cut`, `listings_cut` and a file's `ranges_cut` say a cap cut the list. `range_bytes` counts every range whatever the cap, and the run's own counts are never cut;
  - a cut is never read as "not served". With `files_cut` false, a file without a row was not served; with it true, a file without a row is unknown, since a cap may have left it out, and never not served or served 0 bytes. In a row whose `ranges_cut` is true, every byte below the end of its last listed range is known (inside a range it was served, in a gap it was not), and a byte past that end is unknown. Directories follow the rule of files with `listings_cut`. `served.lookup_file(snapshot, file_id)`, `lookup_directory(snapshot, dir_id)` and `lookup_bytes(snapshot, file_id, start, stop)` (a claimed read of `[start, stop)`) apply exactly this rule to a snapshot (the instrument's, or the sandbox's `served_bytes()`): each returns a `Lookup` whose `state` is `served`, `not_served` or `unknown`, with `reasons` (`files_cut`, `listings_cut`, `ranges_cut`, `ranges_unknown`, or the run's own `unknown` reasons) and the file's or directory's `row` when it has one. A snapshot that is not one of this version raises `ValueError`;
- what it does not count: headers and TLS framing, what the gate read upstream (so the pages behind a merged listing are not counted, only the answer the agent got), the plain TCP forwards of the knowledge plane, and anything the agent read from its own disk. A count of what was served can exceed what the agent's code consumed by what sat unread in its socket when it stopped reading; the gate log's cut and close lines show how much;
- per data path: a REST run is counted from the gate's log; a mount's reads go through oneclient, which no gate sees, so every count is `None` with `unknown: ["mount_not_through_gate"]`; a run with no data path read nothing through one, so every count is 0 and every table empty;
- the run's start (O24): an executor that checks the sandbox before the agent's loop, as the agent's user (`exec(code, timeout_s, record=False)`, a harness step), calls `sandbox.mark_run_start()` once its last such step has returned and before the loop starts. A REST data path's gate, signalled with SIGUSR1, writes a `run_start` line in its log that names each request it had begun and not yet logged, and the sandbox reads it back before it returns; an agent's cell entered meanwhile waits until it has, so no request of the agent's can precede it. The counts, the tables and so the lookups then hold only the answers after the mark; the answers before it, and those it names, are counted apart in `before_run_start` (`requests`, `served_bytes`, `content_bytes`, `listing_bytes`, `other_bytes`, `gate_answer_bytes`), never in a table, so nothing is hidden. `run_start_marked` says whether a mark was set: without one every answer is the run's, as before, and `before_run_start` is `None`; with no data path it is all zeros. A snapshot recorded before these two keys existed lacks them, which reads as not recorded, never as no mark. `mark_run_start` returns what it found, which `metadata()["run_start"]` keeps: `{"gate": false}` without a gate, otherwise `gate`, `signalled` and `confirmed`. It is refused a second time, once one of the agent's cells has begun (its `exec` entered: a mark during the agent's first cell would set that cell's reads apart), while a harness step runs, and after the collection; a mark that was not confirmed leaves the count unknown, and the executor decides whether the run goes on. The gate's own summary (`metadata()["gate"]`) and its refusals still cover its whole log;
- unknown, never zero: every count and every table is `None`, with the reasons in `unknown`, before the collection (`gate_not_collected`) and whenever a line may be missing or unreadable (`gate_not_stopped`, `gate_teardown_unconfirmed`, `gate_log_unreadable`, `gate_log_malformed`, `gate_log_write_failed`, `gate_output_unreadable`, `gate_teardown_incomplete`, `request_in_flight_at_stop`), so a table is never a part of the run's reads taken for the whole. So is a mark of the run's start the sandbox set and the log does not confirm (`run_start_unconfirmed`): one not read back before the loop started, or not in the log exactly once and before its `teardown_complete` line, is never taken as "everything after"; a `run_start` line in the log of a run that set none, or one that names no requests in the gate's form, is a malformed line. An answer of a file's content or a listing whose path names no file or directory is a malformed line. A log counts as whole only with a positive mark, the `teardown_complete` line the gate writes last, once every connection's close line is written: an exit code proves nothing, since a gate killed by its memory limit exits too. The gate's own output (`docker logs`) must also have been read, since it is where a failed write of the log shows; a read that fails leaves the count unknown. The sandbox's `metadata()["served_bytes"]` holds the same count for operators.

The count is taken once, when the collection stops the gate, and the record keeps it: replay and re-scoring read the recorded snapshot, tables included, and never measure them again.

The third is `expcore_onedata.ingest.ProgressiveIngest(provisioner, plan, *, journal, max_wait_s=1.0, join_timeout_s=None)` (namespace `progressive_ingest`, `progressive_ingest_space<i>` with several spaces, O27): part of a dataset arrives during the run, as if its owner were still ingesting it, so a study can test whether an agent notices that its data are incomplete. In the arrival mode a file is absent until it arrives; in the preallocated mode (O44) it is there from the start at its final size, as in the baseline, and only partly written. The plan is a TOML file of the study's, one per condition, loaded with `expcore_onedata.ingestplan.load_ingest_plan(path, config)`:

```toml
[ingest]
space = "<label>"                # the [[spaces]] entry; optional with one space
staging = "staged"               # a local directory, relative to the plan, holding every source
visible_on = ["<provider>"]      # optional: who must see each arrival; by default every provider of the space
mode = "arrival"                 # optional: "arrival" (the default) or "preallocated" (O44)

[[ingest.files]]
label = "day-13"                 # how records and messages name the file, never by its path
path = "records/day-13.csv"      # where the agent finds it, relative to the space root
source = "day-13.csv"            # its content, relative to staging; hashed at load and never changed
at_s = 30                        # seconds after the schedule's anchor: the instrument's start, or the first turn
baseline = "complete"            # required: "complete" (part of the dataset between runs) or "absent" (new data)
parts = 4                        # optional: written in parts, so it is partial for a while (1, whole, by default)
over_s = 60                      # with parts > 1: the last part this many seconds after the first
prefix = 65536                   # the preallocated mode only, and required there: the bytes written at the start
replicas = ["<provider>"]        # the preallocated mode only, optional: who else holds the file whole in the baseline
```

Each refusal is a `ConfigError` naming the plan's file and the key: an unknown key, a label or path given twice, a path under another, a source outside the staging directory, `parts` above the source's bytes after the prefix, `over_s` without parts or parts without it, a `visible_on` provider that does not support the space, a `mode` that is neither, a `prefix` in an arrival plan, and in a preallocated plan a missing `prefix`, one that is not a whole number from 0 to below the source's size, and a `baseline` other than "complete"; `replicas` outside a preallocated plan, or naming a provider twice or one that does not support the space (one that is the data provider is refused when the instrument is built, `ValueError`). `IngestPlan.steps()` lists every part in the order it falls due, part k at `at_s + over_s * (k - 1) / (parts - 1)`, starting where the one before it ended (`start`, the first at `prefix`, 0 in an arrival plan) and ending at byte `prefix + ceil((size - prefix) * k / parts)`, with the SHA-256 of everything before its end; `PlannedFile.prefix_sha256` is the prefix's. The plan is part of the condition and of the campaign fingerprint: `StudyPlugin(platform={**config.fingerprint(), **ingestplan.fingerprint({condition: plan})})` holds one hash per plan, covering its space, its providers and each file's path, staged content, schedule and baseline, and in a preallocated plan its mode, prefixes and replicas, so a resume after any of them changed is refused (C06); an arrival plan keeps the hash it had before the mode existed. An empty plan loads and records the same steps, for a sham control, in either mode.

The executor gives the instrument a provisioner of its own, bound to the plan's space, whose change log is the ingest's and is never given to a teardown, and a journal file kept across runs. The instrument claims the log when it is built (`Provisioner.claim_log`, a line of the log): a log that already holds another user's creation or another role's claim is refused with `ProvisionError`, and `teardown` over a claimed log is refused with nothing sent; the ingests of one study's runs, one after another, share the claim. The executor starts the instrument with the others and finishes it with `finish_instruments`, which runs its post-run check:
- `start` restores the baseline first, which also recovers a crashed run (a part half written is finished from its staged source into the file the ingest made; a file removed and never restored is written back once every provider answers its old id absent), checks that every "absent" file's staged source still has the plan's hash (a "complete" one's restore checked its own) and every planned file's directory exists, and then removes each "complete" file through the gate (its staged copy checked again just before the delete, so a copy damaged between the restore and the removal leaves the file in place and refuses the start), confirmed absent on every provider of the space before its name can be written again (25.0 deletes a directory asynchronously and can lose files under a name deleted and recreated at once, #29; the wait covers every removed file as a precaution). It then starts the schedule's thread. A start that cannot do this restores what it can and raises `InfraError("ingest_failure")`, so the attempt is set aside before the agent runs; any other exception from it is a defect.
- each part is written through the provisioner's `upload(path, source, stop=<its end>)`, so through the mutation gate bound to the plan's space and never in another; it counts only when the hash read back is the plan's hash of that prefix, the data provider's block map covers it, and every provider of `visible_on` shows the file at that size (`consistency.wait_visible`, bounded). A part that cannot be written or confirmed is an ingest failure: the schedule stops and every later part is recorded as `abandoned`, never dropped.
- `finish` stops the schedule (a part not yet due is `not_due`: the run ended first, which is the manipulation, never a failure), restores the baseline and returns the snapshot; `restore()` is the same for an executor's `finally` when `finish` was not reached, and is idempotent; `stop()` only stops the schedule, returning False when a part was still being written at `join_timeout_s` (`[placement] transfer_timeout_s` by default), which is an ingest failure, and the restore then refuses to race it. The restore puts each "complete" file back from its staged source, checked against the plan's hash first, verified by size, SHA-256, the data provider's block map and every `visible_on` provider, and removes each "absent" file. It restores each file on its own: a file that fails stops none of the others, the failures are raised together after the loop (`restore.files` holds each file's result, a failed one with `ok` false), so one bad staged copy never leaves another file missing. `finish` and `restore()` block, so an executor on an event loop calls `restore()` in its `finally` through `asyncio.to_thread`, as it calls `finish_instruments`. It removes a file at a planned path only when the ingest's change log says it made it or its bytes read back are its staged source's, and every removal checks that file's staged source against the plan's hash first, a file the ingest made included, since the copy it came from may be the last good one of its bytes: a damaged copy leaves the file in place and fails that file's restore (or the start, which then restores what it can), an infrastructure failure the post-run check refuses, while the other files are still restored; anything else there is refused with nothing sent, and no directory is created or removed, so no data the study keeps is lost.
- `check()`, the post-run check (C31), raises `InfraError("ingest_failure")` naming the step (the start, an arrival with its label and part, the stop, or the restore) when it failed or never ran, and never a private name; `finish_instruments` puts the refusal in the record as `instrument_refusal` and the scheduler sets the attempt aside as charged infrastructure and runs it again, so a failed ingest is never scored as the agent's outcome, whatever the executor does after. It raises `RuntimeError` for a defect of the schedule, so the attempt is a harness defect. `raise_if_failed()` is the same check, a second guard an executor may call once the record is written. `ingest_failure` is registered with `treatable=False` in `expcore_onedata.sandbox.kinds`: a plan the harness could not carry out says nothing about the agent, while a read of a file that has not arrived is the agent's outcome.
- **The preallocated mode** (`mode = "preallocated"`, O44, `expcore_onedata.ingestsparse.SparseBaseline`) keeps the namespace as in the baseline: each planned file, every one of them "complete", exists at its final size from the start with only its first `prefix` bytes written at the data provider, and the rest arrives in parts. After the restore, the start takes each file in turn from complete to that state: it removes the file as above (its staged copy checked, the intent journalled, its old id answered absent on every provider before the name is used again), makes a new file under the name with `Provisioner.preallocate(path, size)`, which creates it and **extends it first**, by an empty write at offset `size`, and then writes the prefix at offset 0 with `Provisioner.write_range`. Extending first keeps the namespace size final from the first moment the file holds a byte, since every later write lies inside it, where writing the prefix first would show the file at the prefix's size until the extension; the start state is confirmed before the schedule can begin. There is no other way on 25.0 to take a file the data provider holds whole to a prefix: REST truncates only to 0 and the data provider's copy is never evicted (O09), so the start recreates the file, its new id recorded with the old one, and the reset never removes it. **The schedule is anchored at the agent's first model turn**: the instrument has the first-turn hook (`at_first_turn`, C31) in this mode alone, which the executor calls through `first_turn_instruments` just before the loop starts, and the parts' `at_s` count from there; the prefix is written at the start. A run whose hook never came has written no part, which is no manipulation, so `check()` refuses it. **Every state is confirmed** before the next step, as an arrival is: the prefix's SHA-256 read back through the data provider (`read_file(stop=)`), every `visible_on` provider showing the file at its final size, and, through each of them on consecutive polls, `GET /data/{id}/distribution` showing the data provider holding exactly the written ranges at `virtualSize` the final size and no provider a byte beyond them, every provider the answer lists read, one platform.toml does not declare included; a byte beyond them fails at once. Before each part the data provider's block map must be exactly the ranges written so far, so a part out of order, or a file another writer changed, fails closed with nothing written. A part is written at its offsets (`write_range`) and counts once its prefix's hash and the block maps say so; a failure is `ingest_failure` as in the arrival mode. **The restore completes each file in place and never removes it**: the ranges the data provider's block map lacks are written from the staged source, checked first, then the whole file is read back by its hash and written whole again at its offsets when that differs, and it is verified by size, SHA-256, the data provider's block map and every `visible_on` provider (`restore.files[label].action`: `kept`, `completed` or `rewritten`; `created` for a name a crashed start removed, written back once every provider answers the old id absent). A file at the path the ingest did not make is kept when its bytes are its staged source's, the dataset's own before a first run, and refused otherwise with nothing written; the one exception is an empty file at a path whose journal ends with the start's intent to make it again, a creation whose answer was lost or whose change-log line a crash cut, which is removed (it holds nothing; the removal is journalled first, so one that fails is tried again by the next restore) and the file written back whole.
- **Before anything is removed** the preallocated start checks every planned file's staged copy against the plan's size and SHA-256 (a missing one included), before its restore runs, and, before the first removal, each file's holdings: every provider its `replicas` name must hold it whole by that provider's own block map, and no other provider but the data provider may hold a byte of it, as any provider of the space shows it, since the removal takes every replica with the file. Either refuses the start with nothing removed. **The reset restores each file's replicas**: once the file is whole at its path (its hash and the data provider's block map), it is replicated to each provider its `replicas` name that does not hold it whole (`Provisioner.replicate_file(file_id, size, to_provider)`, by id, never whatever else has the path), and each is verified by that provider's block map on consecutive polls; `restore.files[label].replicas` gives `held` or `replicated` by provider position, and a replication whose map stays short fails that file's restore. A replication that completed short, as one the mirror runs from its lagging dbsync copy of the owner's map right after the reset's write does, is scheduled again within the settle bound (`replicate_file`, below), and `restore.files[label].replications` gives, by the position of each provider replicated to, its `tries`, `transfer_ids` and `converged_s` (absent when no provider needed a replication). So a run that reads through a mirror after a preallocated run starts from the baseline's mirror state.
- **The baseline's marks** (`expcore_onedata.ingesthealth`). The journal records whether the baseline is whole, each line pushed to disk before the step it announces, with the space's position, the `phase`, the plan's hash (`plan`), the writer's pid and boot id (`holder`) and its **owner** (`owner`, `expcore.campaign.liveness.owner()`, O45: the attempt the start ran in, by its campaign and the store's id of its row, and the process by boot id, pid and start time, as the lease pool names a holder), each instrument's first line also syncing the journal's directory and every one above it, so no host crash loses the journal: `open` before the start's restore and its first removal (and again, `phase` `run`, once the start state is set), `unhealthy` when a start, a reset or a recovery fails after that (its `error`, `detail`, and for a start the `file` and `step`: `holdings`, `removal`, `creation`, `prefix` or `confirmation`), and `whole` when a restore verifies every planned file at its path and on its replicas. The start also takes the baseline's **hold** (`expcore_onedata.ingesthold`): an exclusive `flock` on `<journal>.<space>.hold`, taken under the admission lock before it writes `open` and let go after a restore writes `whole`, when the attempt that took it ends (`expcore.campaign.liveness.on_end`), when the instrument keeping it is collected, and by the kernel with its process; any process tests it with a shared lock it gives back at once (`hold_taken`). An `open` mark is **held** while its hold is taken and its owner is not shown ended (`ingesthealth.read_mark`, `is_held`) and **orphaned**, as unhealthy as an `unhealthy` mark, the moment no live attempt owns it: its attempt has ended (the store holds its result, finished or set aside), its process is gone, its hold was let go (its attempt ended in another process, or its instrument was dropped) or it has none (a mark written before holds existed); nothing waits for the run's ceiling. An open mark whose hold is free is read again once, since a restore writes `whole` before it lets the hold go. A hold that cannot be read (its file cannot be opened or locked) is unknown, never free: the start is held back and the check fails, as for an orphaned mark, and a recovery is refused, since no run is shown over. **A start is held back**, with nothing read or sent and `InfraError("ingest_held")`, unless the space's last mark is `whole` or there is none, read again under the journal's admission lock (`<journal>.lock`, an exclusive `flock`) just before it writes `open`, so no two starts pass one `whole` mark: a held baseline (its detail says the start waits for the run's reset), an orphaned one and an `unhealthy` one (their detail asks for `recover()`). `ingest_held` is registered `treatable=False, charged=False`, so the attempt is set aside and costs its run no attempt, however often (O45); before it, the refusal was `ingest_failure`, charged, so every refused start used an attempt. A start that changed the baseline and failed is still `ingest_failure`, charged. `check()` after a held start refuses with `ingest_held` too, so an executor that swallowed the start's error is not charged either. A failed start marks the baseline `unhealthy` before its own recovery runs, and that recovery, when it verifies every file, marks it `whole`, as a fault injector's verified clear settles an injection; a refused start changed nothing, and its `finish` and `restore()` bring nothing back (`restore` is `{"ok": true, "files": {}, "untouched": true}`). **`recover()`** on an instrument that has not started is the way back from a failure the start could not settle, or from a run that died: it restores every planned file as the reset does, the replicas included, marks the baseline `whole` and returns `{"ok", "seconds", "files", "id_map"}`, or marks it `unhealthy` and raises `InfraError("ingest_failure")`; it runs under the admission lock, is refused for a mark another plan wrote (the run that left the baseline is the one whose files and replicas it must verify, so it runs with that plan) and for a held baseline, a run in progress, unless this process took it outside any attempt (an instrument it never restored), and must run with no run on the plan's space. **The health check reads the marks**: `PlatformHealth(..., ingest_journals=[...])` fails, before it asks the federation anything, while a space's last mark is `unhealthy` or an orphaned `open`, naming the space by position, with `charge=False` (O45): the gate closes and holds every run, the one that found it included, charging none, since the attempt that left the baseline was charged already, until a check passes after `recover()`. A held `open` mark is a run in progress: a check outside any attempt (the pre-run check, the dispatcher's own) is deferred, also with `charge=False`, so the campaign holds its runs, charging none, and asks again after `recheck_s`, until the run's reset; a check inside an attempt (a post-run check) reads it healthy, unless the mark's owner is that same attempt, whose ingest then ended without its reset, which fails the attempt's own post-run check. A failure of one space wins over a deferral of another. A journal that cannot be read fails it, charged as any failed check; a torn last line is skipped. Give the health check the journals: without them a held start is still uncharged, but it is an attempt row (`ingest_held`) each `retry_delay_s` instead of a wait before any attempt. The failure charges only the attempt that wrote the failing mark, in its own post-run check (`charge=True`), which reports its own failure before any other attempt's; any other attempt whose post-run check meets it is set aside uncharged. So a crash at any point, the removal, the creation, the extension, the prefix, mid-plan or mid-reset, leaves the federation unhealthy, never a sparse or missing baseline in place unflagged, until the recovery ends with the complete file. The arrival mode writes no marks and keeps its recovery at the next start.

The snapshot (version 1) is `{"version", "ok", "anchor", "anchor_clock", "providers", "plan", "start", "steps", "ids", "id_map", "start_s", "files", "failure", "defect", "restore", "reads_retried"}`, with `space` too when several spaces are declared. `reads_retried` is how many of the ingest's reads timed out and were tried again (O29, "A read that timed out is tried again" under the provisioner's files in and out below), which says how many of an ingest's reads needed it. Every reading is clock-labelled: `clock` is the provisioner's monotonic clock, the one the loop's clock readings use, `t_rel_s` the seconds from `anchor_clock`, and `utc`. `anchor` names the schedule's zero: `instrument_start`, the end of `start`, in the arrival mode, and `first_turn` in the preallocated mode, whose snapshot also holds `first_turn` (its `clock`, the run's clock reading the hook was given, `run_clock_s`, the seconds from the end of the start, `after_start_s`, and `utc`; None when the hook never came). `steps` holds each part as `planned` (its `due_s`, `bytes` and `sha256`), `written` (`late_s`, `action`, `file_id`; in the preallocated mode `start` and `sent` instead of `action`), `confirmed` (`holder_covered`, `visible_s` per provider position; in the preallocated mode `size`, `blocks`, the written ranges, `visible_s` and `map_s`, the seconds the block maps took), `failed`, `abandoned` or `not_due`. In the preallocated mode `start.files` holds each file's verified start state by label: `baseline_id`, `file_id` (the file the start made), `size`, `blocks`, `visible_s` and `map_s`. `ids` holds every file id the ingest removed, created or restored, recorded as soon as the file exists (a restore's before its hash is checked and its arrival confirmed; a file an upload made before it failed, found in the change log, as `created`), each with the file's `label`, `event` (`removed`, `created`, `restored`), `phase` (`start`, `run`, `restore`), `old_id`, `new_id`, the restore's `action`, and the `mtime` and `ctime` the data provider gives (None when it gives none, `attributes_ok` false when they could not be read), so a study can map gate errors to files and spaces after the run; the journal holds the same lines, a crashed run's included. `id_map` maps each id a planned file had to the id the ingest gave it in its place, in the order the changes came, for every file it made again (in the preallocated mode each file's baseline id to the id the start made, which the run's reads and the reset use): the map a study keyed by file id follows, as a data gate's rows are. `start_s` is the start's seconds (`start.seconds`), spent before the first turn and so outside the agent's time. `files` holds per label the `baseline`, the `state` (`arrived`, `partial`, `not_arrived`, `failed`; `prefix_only` in place of `not_arrived` in the preallocated mode), `parts_confirmed`, and the `baseline_id`, `arrived_id` (in the preallocated mode the file the start made) and `restored_id`. `plan` is `IngestPlan.summary()`: its hash, its `mode`, and each file's label, bytes, SHA-256, schedule, baseline and `prefix` (0 in the arrival mode). Providers are named by their position and files by their label, never by a name or a path. `ok` is what `check()` passes: false whenever the start did not set the start state, a preallocated schedule never began, or `failure`, `defect` or a failed `restore` is set.

**Binding a snapshot to its plan** (`expcore_onedata.ingestbind.check_recorded_plan(plan, snapshot)`, #262, O27, O44). A study that reads a run's arrivals relative to a plan must first show that the snapshot was recorded under that plan; the core keeps no reader of its own, so a study's checker (its `recorded_arrival`, say) calls this before it reads an arrival time. Matching by the files' labels alone accepts any plan that names the same files, one whose every part is 300 s later included. The check compares by content and returns nothing when the snapshot was recorded under exactly `plan`; otherwise it raises `PlanMismatch` (a `ValueError`; `.label` names the file, `.field` the field, either `None` when no one file differs). It settles the plan's hash (`snapshot["plan"]["sha256"]`, which covers the space, `visible_on`, each file's path, staged content, schedule and baseline, and in the preallocated mode the mode, prefixes and replicas) and then names where the plans differ: the `mode`; each file, by label and in the plan's order, by `bytes`, `sha256`, `at_s`, `parts`, `over_s`, `baseline` and `prefix`; and each reading of `steps`, whose kind must be one the ingest makes (`planned`, `written`, `confirmed`, `failed`, `abandoned`, `not_due`) and whose part must be one of the plan's, with its `parts`, `due_s` and, in a `planned`, `written` or `confirmed` reading, its `bytes` (and in a `planned` one its `sha256`), with, once the start set the start state (`start.ok` true), exactly one `planned` reading per part; a `start` that is missing, or is neither none nor a record with a boolean `ok`, is refused, so no malformed start earns the exemption of a start that did not set its state. A hash that differs while every itemised field agrees is refused too, saying the difference lies in what a snapshot does not itemise (the space, `visible_on`, a path or a replica). Nothing missing passes: a snapshot that records no plan, no plan hash (the snapshot has recorded `plan.sha256` since the first version, so none is added), a file without one of its fields or no `steps` is refused with "unknown is not a pass". The two keys a snapshot from before the preallocated mode did not keep, `mode` and a file's `prefix`, read as `arrival` and 0, since every such snapshot came from an arrival plan, whose hash has not changed since (C06): it binds to the arrival plan that made it, and a preallocated plan is refused on `mode`. A refusal names only labels, field names and the plan's and snapshot's numbers, never a path or a name.

**What a read of an unwritten range returns**, which the preallocated mode rests on. Measured on the Compose federation (25.0 development build, op-worker f118ed9): an unwritten range reads as zeros, at once and at its full length, through the REST content endpoint (a 206 with its `Content-Range`, through the data provider and through a mirror once the mirror shows the file) and through the mount, with no error, short read or wait; the block map holds only the written ranges, on every provider, and an empty write at an offset past the end set the size to that offset with no block (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-10-federation-facts.md`, "A file at its final size with only a prefix written", and `2026-11-11-federation-facts-mount.md`, "Unwritten ranges through the mount"). From op-worker f118ed9's source, not measured: the empty write's zero-size block is dropped (read_write_req.erl:58-86, replica_updater.erl:187-192, fslogic_blocks.erl:125-126) and the storage file is extended sparsely; a read of the gap starts no transfer, since no provider holds it (replica_finder.erl:53-89); through the mount each read into the gap costs oneclient about seven `SynchronizeBlock` round trips before it returns zeros (oneclient bb8a63e3, fsLogic.cc:952-1001); a write of content without `offset` truncates the file to 0 first (file_content_rest_handler.erl:224-228), so the client refuses one before it is sent (`TruncatingWrite`, under "The platform layer's files in and out"). Only the block map tells an unwritten range from written zeros, and the agent's tools may not show it.

Not yet in it: #27's sanction, durable recovery (the preallocated mode's marks and `recover()` aside, which a person runs) and exclusive admission with its matched control, so a study that runs ingest and other conditions on one owner's space must keep them apart itself, and optional metadata signals of an ingest in progress. The arrival mode ran on the Compose federation on 12 Nov, and every item below held (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-progressive-ingest.md`); the preallocated mode has not run live, and its gated test is `tests/live/test_ingest_preallocated.py` (`EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`). That check must show: a new file extended by the empty write before any byte of it is written has the final size and no block on any provider; its prefix is all the data provider's block map holds; a read of the gap through every provider answers zeros and places no block; and the instrument sets that start state, writes its parts after the first turn with each confirmed, and completes the file in place at the reset with its id kept. A live check of the arrival mode on the Compose federation, with two providers supporting the space, must show: a "complete" file removed at `start` answers `posix` `enoent` on both providers before the run begins; at its offset it appears on the data provider and, within the visibility bound, on the other provider at its size, and a file in parts is seen there at each part's size in turn; every recorded file id answers `GET /data/{id}` while it exists and resolves to the plan's space by its parent links, and the removed ones answer absent; the clock readings fall in the plan's order with their lateness; after `finish` the file has its staged source's SHA-256, the data provider's block map covers it, and the staged copy and every other file of the space are unchanged; a name removed and written again after the absence check never loses the new file; and a refused write (a read-only token, say) is recorded as `ingest_failure`.

The fourth is `expcore_onedata.sandbox.iomode.MountIoMode(sandbox, *, refuse=True)` (namespace `mount_io_mode`, O49): the mount's declared and effective oneclient I/O mode, so a study can confirm from each run's record that every mount run ran in the declared mode (O36). Build it once `SandboxPlatform.launch` has yielded the run's sandbox, list it with the others, and call `sandbox.collect(...)` before `finish_instruments`: the collection reads the sidecar's oneclient INFO log once (`DockerSandbox.mount_io_mode`; the reading is also in the metadata as `mount.effective`, kept even when a direct mount's own check refuses the collection), and `finish` records it. It is `check`ed after every snapshot, and refuses (below). The snapshot is `{"version": 1, "mount": true, "declared", "effective", "agree", "forced_direct", "forced_proxy", "lines", "problems"}`, with nothing of the log in it:
- `declared` is `[sandbox.mount] io_mode`, `proxy` or `direct`, never detected. `effective` is what oneclient's own log shows: `direct` or `proxy` when its start lines `Forced direct IO` and `Forced proxy IO` force that mode (`1` and `0`, `0` and `1`) and no line shows a read that did not follow it; `auto` when neither is forced (oneclient's own detection); `mixed` when the log contradicts itself, both forced, a start line logged again with another value, or a forced mode followed by such a line; `unknown` when the log could not be read or a start line is missing or, anywhere in the log, is not `0` or `1` (every logged occurrence of a start line counts, not the first alone, and the reading never repeats a value the log holds). The lines that contradict a forced direct start are those of `directio.MARKERS`: oneclient's own detection, a direct read refused for permissions and made again through the provider (a fallback to proxy), a switch to the provider's helper, a storage that is not direct on the provider or that the sidecar could not reach itself; after a forced proxy start, a detection that did not itself force proxy (`forced proxy io mode: 0`). `forced_direct` and `forced_proxy` are the start values, `0` or `1`, None when not logged as one; `lines` the names of the contradicting lines found; `problems` the reasons, in words that name no id, path or host, the first naming both modes when they differ;
- `agree` is true only when `declared` and `effective` are the same word. **Unknown is never agreement**: an unreadable, empty or unparsable log, a snapshot taken before the collection and a log without its start lines all read `unknown`. `agrees(snapshot)` recomputes the answer for a scorer from the two modes (it never takes the flag alone) and returns False for a snapshot that is not a mount snapshot of version 1, a run's with no mount included;
- **the policy: a disagreement is refused.** `check()` raises `InfraError("io_mode_integrity")` for a mount run whose snapshot is not agreement, and when `finish` never ran or failed, naming both modes in its detail (`declared 'proxy', oneclient's start lines show 'direct'`). `io_mode_integrity` is registered `treatable=False` in `expcore_onedata.sandbox.kinds`, so no condition can declare it, and `finish_instruments` writes the refusal as `instrument_refusal` ([below](#refusing-an-attempt-the-first-turn-hook-and-the-post-run-check)): the attempt is set aside as charged infrastructure, run again, and never scored as the declared mode, and it keeps its record, its snapshot and its usage. A study that wants the disagreement in the record without that sets `refuse=False`: the snapshot still says `agree` false, `check` passes, and the study's scorer must confirm the mode with `agrees` before it counts a run as one of the declared mode;
- a run with no mount (a REST run, a run with no data path) has no mode to confirm: the snapshot is `{"version": 1, "mount": false}` and is never refused, so a study may list the instrument for every run;
- the record is unchanged but for the new namespace: no schema field is added, a record of a run without the instrument or without a mount has no `mount_io_mode` key, and the fixtures of version 3 replay byte for byte. **Not run live:** that a forced proxy mount logs no detection line with `forced proxy io mode: 0`, and that a proxy mount's start lines are already in the file when the collection reads it. oneclient buffers INFO lines for up to 30 s unless `--disable-log-buffering` is passed, so the sidecar passes it with every mount, proxy included (O54, in the sidecar's log-directory item below); a run whose start lines are not in the file reads `unknown` and is refused, which the first live proxy run with the instrument shows.

### Egress where it cannot be blocked: the `egress` instrument

`expcore.egress` (O52, #179). Where an agent's network cannot be restricted (an HPC batch node, whose unprivileged users get no network namespace), its outbound use is detected and reported instead. The HPC bundle logs it (`deploy/hpc-federation/bundle/egresslog.c`, a shim in the agent's cell, and `egress-collector.py`, outside it); the core reads the log, classifies it and reports it.

```python
from expcore.egress import EgressLog, declaration

declared = declaration(
    [("127.0.0.1", gate_port)], ("127.0.0.1", gate_port), hosts=["provider-a.test"], network_calls=True
)
instruments = [..., EgressLog(source, declared, nonce)]  # source.egress_log() -> bytes | None
```

- **The declaration** (`Declaration`, recorded in the snapshot): `gate`, the data gate's endpoints as the cell reaches them; `canary`, the endpoint of the launcher's canary connect; `loopback`, the ranges that never leave the node (`127.0.0.0/8` and `::1/128` by default); `hosts`, the platform's names, which a URL in the agent's code may name; `network_calls`, true for a data path that is the agent's own HTTP to the gate (REST). A destination is `gate` (its address and port are a gate endpoint), `loopback` (its address is in a range) or `outside` (everything else: the node's own addresses and the unspecified address included). An IPv4-mapped address is classified as its IPv4 one.
- **The log is intact only when** it is present and under 256 MiB, every line parses as its kind, the first is the start line with this run's `nonce` and the last the end line with nothing after it, `seq` runs from 0 without a gap and `mono` never goes back, the end line counts the lines before it, no line is malformed, none unwritten, no record says its sender dropped one, every record came from the collector's uid, and the first record is the canary's connect. Otherwise it is unknown, with every reason: `log_missing`, `log_empty`, `log_too_large`, `log_unparseable`, `no_start_line`, `nonce_mismatch`, `not_monotonic`, `not_closed`, `end_counts_differ`, `malformed_datagrams`, `unwritten_datagrams`, `records_dropped`, `foreign_sender`, `no_canary`.
- **The snapshot** (version 1): `{"version", "log": "intact" | "unknown", "unknown": [reasons], "declared", "canary", "counts": {"gate", "loopback", "outside"} | None, "records", "outside": [{"address", "port", "family", "type", "attempts", "results"}], "outside_cut", "outside_complete"}`. `counts` are the destinations asked for (a connect, or a send to a new address) and are None whenever the log is unknown: unknown is never zero. `outside` lists each outside destination with its results by errno (0 for a connect that succeeded); for an unknown log it is what the readable part shows, a lower bound. At most 1000 destinations are listed. The canary's records are in none of the counts. The executor stops the collector after the agent's cell and before `finish_instruments`; a collector still running has written no end line, and its log reads as unknown.
- **The static scan** (`scan_cells(code_cells(record), declared)`): every `code` argument of a call to a tool whose input schema has a string `code` property, scanned for a `url` off the platform (recorded by its host only), a `download` (`curl`, `wget`, `pip install`, `git clone`, `conda install`) and a `network_call` (`requests`, `urllib`, `urllib3`, `http.client`, `httpx`, `aiohttp`, `socket`, `ftplib`, `smtplib`, `paramiko`, `pycurl`). A `url` or `download` hit flags; a `network_call` flags unless the declaration says `network_calls`.
- **The verdict** (`judge(record, condition)`): a run is flagged `outside` (its log lists an outside destination), `log_unknown` (the log is unknown, or the record has no snapshot, `not_recorded`, a failed instrument, `instrument_failed`, or a snapshot this code does not read or that contradicts itself, `not_a_snapshot`) or `static_scan`. **A missing or damaged log is never read as clean.**
- **The analysis** (`study.pipeline.analyse_study`): once one analysed record carries the namespace, every analysed run is judged (a run without the snapshot is `not_recorded`), and the preregistered analysis is run again without the flagged runs, each passed as an absent run of reason `excluded`. `StudyAnalysis.egress` holds both (`expcore.egress.sensitivity.EgressAnalysis`: `runs`, `sensitivity`, `refused`); `analysis.json` gains `egress` (counts, each run's verdict, the sensitivity analysis's contrasts and Holm decisions, with each named Holm family's and each declared equivalence family's members and steps under `holm_families` and `equivalence_families` as the primary analysis holds them, or why it was refused); the report gains `## Egress (detected, not prevented)` after the conditions and the shards (`expcore.report_egress`); the terminal summary gains its lines. The sensitivity analysis is shown as the primary one is, after the same checks (`report_families`): with several named Holm families (S17), one table and one set of steps per family under `##### Holm family`, titled "N Holm families, each adjusted on its own" instead of "Holm family of N", and each rerun contrast's terminal line names its family; with equivalence claims (S15, S18), their table, and a declared family's TOST steps, under `####`, without the primary's notes. One flat family has none of that, byte for byte. The mount guard's and both guards' reruns are shown and written the same way. A batch in which no record carries it has none of these, byte for byte.
- **Limits.** Detection, not prevention: a statically linked program, raw system calls, io_uring and the C library's own resolver pass the shim, which is why the scan is the second line; the scan reads text, so code that builds a name or a call at run time passes it. Under one uid the agent can send forged records to the collector, which add lines (a malformed one makes the log unknown) and never remove one.

### The read log: the `read_log` instrument and its channel

`expcore_onedata.readlog` (O91). A study's canonical scripts (the ones its agent's code tool runs inside the sandbox cell to read the data) log every read they make, and the study's omission scoring rests on that log, so the agent must be able neither to read it nor to rewrite or delete it. The scripts send each record to a collector the harness runs outside the cell; the log is the harness's, and a forged append, which cannot be prevented, is detected (the read-log scan; on a REST data path, the gate's per-file served table, `served.lookup_bytes`).

**The fixed names** (`readlog.paths`; no environment variable, so the agent's code cannot point a script at a collector of its making):

| What | Where |
|---|---|
| the socket directory, bound **read-only** into every cell | `/expcore-readlog` |
| the collector's datagram socket | `/expcore-readlog/sock` |
| the client module, bound **read-only**, imported by explicit path (cells run `python -I`) | `/expcore-readlog-client/expcore_readlog_client.py` |
| a collector log line's schema | `expcore-readlog/1` |
| the instrument and its side file's hash field | `read_log`, `instruments.read_log.side_sha256` |

Both targets are harness-owned: a backend adds them to its own targets (the Apptainer settings' `OWN_TARGETS`) and to `sandbox.inputs.TAKEN`, so a study can neither bind nor name an input at either. A Unix datagram socket takes a send on a read-only bind, as the egress shim's does.

**The client's contract** (`readlog.client`, standard library only, Python 3.8 or later; one file, hashed into the run's facts):

```python
import importlib.util as u

path = "/expcore-readlog-client/expcore_readlog_client.py"
spec = u.spec_from_file_location("expcore_readlog_client", path)
readlog = u.module_from_spec(spec)
spec.loader.exec_module(readlog)


def read_file(item, start, stop):
    data = fetch(item, start, stop)  # the script's own read of the data
    readlog.log_read(item, range=[start, stop], bytes=len(data), sha256=sha256_hex(data))  # BEFORE returning it
    return data  # a log_read that raised never reaches this line: no data is returned, the read is "unread"
```

- **Log before you return data, and return no data when the call raises.** Every failure raises `ReadLogError`: the socket is missing (ENOENT), nothing listens (ECONNREFUSED), the collector's queue stays full past `SEND_TIMEOUT_S` (2 s), the collector has stopped reading (EPIPE), a field is not one the collector accepts, or the line is longer than 4,096 bytes of ASCII JSON (checked before anything is sent). The log is then an upper bound on what the scripts handed out.
- **One datagram per record.** The line is `{"v": 1, "op": "read" | "canary", "item", ["range": [start, stop]], ["bytes"], ["sha256"]}`; `item` is the study's id of what was read (1 to 1,024 characters, never a host path), `range` is `[start, stop]` with `stop` excluded, `bytes` and `sha256` describe what was returned.
- **A send that succeeded only means "queued at the collector".** A later loss (the collector killed with records queued, the log past its cap, a malformed line) makes the run's whole log unknown, never a run with fewer reads.
- The harness's canary, `log_canary()`, is the log's first record, sent before the agent runs.
- **A platform that declared no channel fails closed.** It binds neither target: importing the client by its fixed path raises `FileNotFoundError`, and a copy of the client that a study vendored raises `ReadLogError` (no socket, ENOENT). A script treats both as unread and returns no data, so a platform template that forgot the declaration shows at the first smoke cell, not as runs with silently missing logs. Each backend's tests hold that nothing is bound without the declaration.
- **Run each canonical script as its own process** (`python -I <script>`), never imported into the agent's own process: patching the client or `socket` then needs code in the script's process, which only the script's own source has. Keep the scripts in a read-only input.

**The collector** (`readlog.collector`, a single file the harness runs as `python3 -I collector.py <socket dir> <log file> <nonce> <expected peer uid> [<max log bytes>]`, a sibling of the egress collector that imports neither it nor the package). It prints `readlog collector ready` once it is listening; stamps each line with `seq`, `wall`, `mono` (its own receive time, comparable with the cells' times on the same host) and the sender's pid and uid (SCM_CREDENTIALS); writes a start line (the nonce, the collector's uid, the expected peer uid) and, on SIGTERM, an end line with the counts, after it has stopped receiving (so a send that succeeded was queued before the stop and is drained, and a later one raises) and read what was queued; writes its log `O_EXCL` 0600; keeps the log under a byte cap (default 48 MiB, below the side-file cap) and counts what it could not write (`unwritten`, `cap_reached`); and writes a datagram that is no client line, was cut (`MSG_TRUNC`) or does not parse as a malformed line, by size and SHA-256 only, never its bytes. **The expected peer uid** is an argument because it differs by backend: the driver's own on Apptainer (the socket directory 0700), the declared agent uid on Docker (the directory 0755 and the socket 0666, so that uid can connect). The peer's pid across PID namespaces reads 0 on Docker; it is recorded and never judged.

**The reader** (`readlog.reader.read_log(data, nonce, expected_peer_uid=)`): the log is **intact** only with a start line of this run (its nonce, the expected peer uid), `seq` from 0 without a gap and `mono` never going back, the end line last with counts equal to the lines before it, no malformed line, no datagram left unwritten and no cap reached, the harness's canary as the first record, and every record's sender the expected uid. Otherwise it is **unknown**, with every reason (`log_missing`, `log_empty`, `log_too_large`, `log_unparseable`, `no_start_line`, `nonce_mismatch`, `peer_uid_mismatch`, `not_monotonic`, `not_closed`, `end_counts_differ`, `malformed_datagrams`, `unwritten_datagrams`, `cap_reached`, `foreign_sender`, `bad_record`, `no_canary`), and the records before the first damaged line are kept as a lower bound. **A run with an unknown log reads as unknown reads, never as fewer reads.**

```python
from expcore_onedata.readlog.instrument import ReadLog

instruments = [..., ReadLog(source, nonce, expected_peer_uid)]  # source.read_log() -> bytes | None
```

**The instrument** (`ReadLog`, namespace `read_log`) takes the log's bytes, which the sandbox read once after it stopped the collector, as `EgressLog` takes its. Its snapshot: `{"version": 1, "log": "intact" | "unknown", "unknown": [reasons], "canary", "counts": {"reads", "canaries"} | None, "records", "side_sha256", "side_bytes"}`. `counts` is None whenever the log is unknown: unknown is never zero. `records` is the reads the readable part shows, a lower bound for an unknown log. A log that is missing has no side file (`side_sha256` None); one past `MAX_SIDE_FILE_BYTES` is unknown (`side_file_too_large`). **The run's facts** (through the one writer of `infrastructure_facts`, owner `readlog`, key `read_log`; `readlog.facts.fact`): the SHA-256 of the collector and of the client, where the backend ran the collector (`apptainer_helper`, `docker_container`) and the expected peer uid.

**The log as the study's side file.** The study declares `[[study.side_files]] pattern = "read_log.jsonl"`, `hash_field = "instruments.read_log.side_sha256"` (`paths.SIDE_FILE_NAME`, `paths.SIDE_HASH_FIELD`). The log was never in the sandbox's `/work`, so the executor places it from where the collection left it on the harness's disk, with `stage_harness_side_file(request, side_files, name, path)` beside `stage_side_file`: the same temporary dot-name, hashing as it copies, fsync, the record that names `staged.sha256`, then `staged.commit()`. It returns None when no file lies at `path`, refuses a link or a file over `MAX_SIDE_FILE_BYTES`, and never renames a file whose hash the record does not name.

**Limits.** Detection, not prevention: an agent's own code can call the client or the socket directly and forge a read (the read-log scan reads the code the agent ran for those routes, and on REST the served table catches claimed reads never served), and an agent that reads the data some other way than through the scripts leaves no record (a study's design question: such reads score as omissions). **Code that runs in a script's own process can also hide a real read:** it can patch the client module or the standard library's `socket` before the script logs, so the data is returned and nothing is recorded, a false omission that no log check can see (run each script as its own process to close this route; the read-log scan is a heuristic over the text of the agent's code, which matches the channel's names, Unix datagram sockets, ways to find the socket and patches of the socket or the client, and which code built at run time passes; on a REST data path the gate's per-file served table, which the agent cannot reach, is the backstop and catches a served read the log lacks). The channel is Linux only (a Unix socket cannot cross a bind on Docker for macOS).

**Tool servers.** The log is out of the agent's reach only if no tool the agent can call takes a local host path. The run's tool server is a process of the driver's, outside every cell (on the host, outside every container, on Docker); on the Apptainer backend it runs on the node as the same uid as the collector, beside the run's `readlog/` and `egress/` directories, whose 0700 modes keep out other users and not that uid. A tool that reads, lists or writes a path of the node the agent can name would reach the log, or the egress log, however the cells are bound. A study that pins a tool server, or changes the set of tools it exposes, therefore checks the argument schema of every exposed tool for a host-path argument (a path, a file name or a directory the server resolves on the node), and keeps a held test of that check with its manifests, which hold each tool's input schema ([the frozen manifest](#the-frozen-manifest)), so a manifest frozen again cannot add one unseen. A tool that takes an Onedata logical path or a file id and resolves it over REST is fine: it names data of the federation, never a file of the node.

### The mount on HPC, guarded: the `mount_guard` instrument

`expcore.mountguard` and the Apptainer backend's mount helper (O60, #179). On an HPC batch node the agent's cells and oneclient run as one uid, so the token a mount needs cannot be kept from the agent as the Docker sidecar keeps it. The mount path stays there with detection and report, as for egress: oneclient runs on the node outside every cell, a watcher outside the cells logs what touches its token or its process and proves each cell isolated from both, and the core reads that log.

```python
from expcore.mountguard import mount_guard_for
from expcore_onedata.sandbox.apptainer.hostmount import host_mount_factory

factory = host_mount_factory()  # its oneclient: the bundle [sandbox.apptainer] oneclient_bundle pins (O65)
platform = SandboxPlatform(..., sandbox_options={"mount_helper": factory})  # or ApptainerSandbox(..., mount_helper=)
instruments = [..., mount_guard_for(sandbox)]  # for every run: its guard if it has a mount, else the statement (O75)
```

`mount_guard_for(sandbox)` is `MountGuard(sandbox, sandbox.mount_guard_nonce)` for a run whose `sandbox.data_path_kind` is `mount` (the log is `sandbox.mount_watch_log()`, bytes or None), and `NoMount(kind)` for `rest` and `none`, so a campaign that mixes data paths lists one instrument for every run. A mount run whose helper keeps no watcher raises `ValueError`; it is never taken for a run with no mount.
- **The helper** (`apptainer.hostmount.HostMount`, `mounthook.MountHelper`). `token_paths()` names the token's file, which the sandbox lists in the run's record before `start()` writes it (a path outside the run's directory is refused before the start), so the teardown and the reaper of a driver killed outright remove it before any other step, even one that fails; the reaper removes only a listed path inside the run's directory, never through a link (each directory is opened without following one), and the sandbox refuses a path that leaves the run by `..` or goes through a link, even one that stays in the run. `start()` makes `<run>/mount-guard/` (0700) with `token/`, `oneclient-log/`, `oneclient-home/`, `watch/` and `oneclient-bundle/`, none under a path a cell binds; unpacks oneclient's bundle into `oneclient-bundle/` (O65, below) and runs its `--version` there; writes the token to `token/token` (0600); starts oneclient through `sh -c` that reads the file into `ONECLIENT_ACCESS_TOKEN` and becomes `env -u ONECLIENT_INSECURE -u ONECLIENT_OVERRIDE -u ONECLIENT_CONFIG HOME=<oneclient-home> [SSL_CERT_FILE=<copy>] <launcher> -f -H <provider> --port <port> -o ro <force-I/O> --config /dev/null --disable-log-buffering [--insecure] --log-dir <oneclient-log> <extra_args> <run>/mount`, without `allow_other`, which a node without `user_allow_other` refuses; waits for this uid's read-only FUSE mount; starts the watcher; removes the token file (verified); and returns `<run>/mount` once the watcher has opened the agent's window. The sandbox binds that directory read-only at `[sandbox.mount] path` in every cell. Refused at the start: a direct mount (its check reads a file from a cell before any exists, and a provider's storage is on another node; HPC mounts are proxy-only until the direct-I/O comparison passes), an extra argument that sets the port, a platform that declares no bundle, and a bundle that is not the pinned file or holds what a bundle may not; a `--version` that does not name oneclient's version fails it (`SandboxError`), before the token is written. `stop()` closes the window, unmounts (`fusermount3 -u`, then oneclient's SIGTERM, then a lazy unmount), shows oneclient gone, the mount out of the table and the token file gone, removes any file of oneclient's log that holds the token (`log_files_scrubbed`), and removes the bundle's copy once oneclient is shown gone; it raises `SandboxError` naming each step that failed, and a second call does what is left. `metadata()` holds `io_mode` (the reading of oneclient's log, `sandbox.iomode`, taken when the window closes; `not collected` before), `oneclient: "node"`, `oneclient_build` (`{"version", "fuse", "build_id", "bundle_sha256"}`: oneclient's own `--version` words, the binary's GNU build id and the bundle's pin; None when the start did not reach them; the Docker sandbox records the same keys with `image` in place of `bundle_sha256`, see `sandbox.oneclient_build`), `options`, `extra_args` (names and hash), `log_dir_from`, `wait_s` and `guard` (`nonce`, `verdict`, `snapshot`; `not_collected` before the window closes). Never a token, a path or a host.
- **oneclient's bundle** (O65; `apptainer.oneclient_bundle`, `deploy/hpc-federation/oneclient_bundle.py`). A batch node has no oneclient of its own, and one run from a SIF mounts in the SIF's mount namespace, not the node's, so the host mount runs a relocatable bundle built from the image the Compose sidecar is pinned to: one oneclient build on both backends. `[sandbox.apptainer]` declares it, `oneclient_bundle = "<tar>"` and `oneclient_bundle_sha256 = "<64 hex>"` (both or neither; required when a data path is a mount, so a platform without it does not load), and `record()` holds `oneclient_bundle_sha256`, so the campaign fingerprint's `sandbox_backend` tells two bundles apart and a platform without one keeps the fingerprint it had. `host_mount_factory()` takes no oneclient: a binary given to it raises `TypeError`, since its runs would carry no pin. `unpack(pin, root)` reads the tar once, hashing every byte, the bytes after the archive's end included, and writes only the directories `bin` and `lib` and the regular files `bin/oneclient`, `lib/<name>` and `PROVENANCE.json` (owner-only, the binary and the loader 0500, the rest 0400); a link, a device, a path that climbs or goes deeper, a name twice, a missing binary or loader, or another SHA-256 refuses the start with nothing left. `Bundle.launcher()` is `lib/ld-linux-x86-64.so.2 --inhibit-cache --inhibit-rpath <every file of the bundle> --library-path lib bin/oneclient`: the image's libraries carry the DT_RPATH `/opt/oneclient/lib`, which glibc searches before `--library-path`, and a library opened at run time (an NSS module) falls back to `/etc/ld.so.cache`; with both off, a name is looked for in the bundle, then only in the loader's own Debian-layout directories (on a batch node on 16 Nov 2026, nothing outside the bundle was tried or opened at `--version`). `build_id` reads the binary's GNU build-id note, since `oneclient --version` prints only `Oneclient: <version>` and `FUSE library: <version>`.
- **The window** opens when both inotify watches (the token file's and its directory's) see the host remove the token file, before any cell starts, and closes when the watcher is stopped: by the collection, once every process of the run is shown gone (it copies the log to `mount-watch.jsonl` and returns `mount_watch_log`), or, at the latest, when the instrument reads `sandbox.mount_watch_log()` or the helper stops. A cell of the run still alive at the close is a failed proof, so a window closed early is never clean.
- **The watcher** (`python -I -B -m expcore_onedata.sandbox.mountwatch`, the driver's interpreter, the driver's uid) writes JSON lines numbered from 0 with `wall` and `mono`: `start` (`format` `expcore-mountwatch/1`, `nonce`, `pid`, `uid`, `nspid_depth`, `ptrace_scope`, `watches`, `mount_seen`), `oneclient` (`pid`, `seen`, `nspid_depth`), `inotify` (`target` `token` or `dir`, `mask` names, `entry` `self`, `token` or `other`, never a name), `overflow`, `armed`, `tracer` (`attached`, on each change of oneclient's `TracerPid`), `sampling` (`stopped`, `gone` or `unreadable`), `proof` (`label` `cell`, `host_process` or `close`; `ok`; `problems`; `cell`: `pid`, `start_time`, `nspid_depth`), `disarmed`, `end` (`lines`, `samples`, `polls`, `polls_failed`, `unwritten`). Every `--poll-s` (50 ms) it lists this uid's processes and looks once at each new one: a process in a nested PID namespace whose mount table holds the run's FUSE device is a cell of the run, proved once per namespace (every process of the uid is looked at again at every poll for as long as it lives, as Apptainer may still be making its mounts when it is first seen, and a problem a later look finds is a failed proof; a process of another uid is read again every second, in case it becomes one of the uid's) (`oneclient_not_host`, `oneclient_unread`, `host_proc_in_cell`, `protected_reachable`, `protected_unresolved`); one of the node's namespace that holds the run's mount anywhere but the host's mount point fails (`same_pid_namespace`); a failed listing, or a nested process of the uid whose mount table cannot be read, is a failed poll.
- **The log is intact only when** it is present and under 64 MiB, every line parses as its kind, the first is the start line of this run's `nonce` with both watches set, `seq` runs from 0 without a gap and `mono` never goes back, the last is the end line counting the lines before it with none unwritten, the canary (`IN_DELETE_SELF` on the token's watch and `IN_DELETE` of its entry on the directory's) and a seen oneclient come before `armed`, `armed` and `disarmed` are there once each and in order, nothing but the end line follows `disarmed`, the end line's polls and samples agree with the lines, and there is no overflow, no proof outside the window and no unreadable sample before the window closes. Otherwise it is unknown, with every reason: `log_missing`, `log_empty`, `log_too_large`, `log_unparseable`, `no_start_line`, `nonce_mismatch`, `not_monotonic`, `not_closed`, `end_counts_differ`, `unwritten_lines`, `watch_not_set`, `no_canary`, `not_armed`, `not_disarmed`, `events_lost`, `oneclient_unwatched`, `sampling_failed`, `proof_outside_window`, `lines_after_window`, `not_sampled`. An access after `disarmed` still counts. **The isolation is proven only when** the run's mount was seen, no poll failed, the window holds at least one proof and none failed (a passing cell proof names a process deeper in PID namespaces than the watcher), and the log was not cut; otherwise it is unproven, with `mount_unseen`, `cell_poll_failed`, `no_proof`, `proof_failed`, `cell_not_nested` and the failed proofs' problems, or `log_incomplete`.
- **The snapshot** (version 1, under `instruments["mount_guard"]`): `{"version", "log": "intact" | "unknown", "unknown": [reasons], "isolation": "proven" | "unproven", "unproven": [reasons], "accesses": {"token_file", "token_dir"}, "traced", "proofs", "ptrace_scope"}`: the events on each watch inside the window, the tracers attached inside it, the proofs read in it and Yama's setting. The counts are a lower bound when the log is unknown. They are not named `token`: the record's redaction takes a key `token` or `..._token` for a secret's. It is an instrument's entry, so the record's schema is unchanged (fixture `08-mount-guard.json`).
- **A run with no mount** (O75: a REST run, a run with no data path). `NoMount(kind)` records `{"version": 1, "mount": false, "data_path": "rest" | "none"}` under the same namespace, and `ApptainerSandbox.data_path_kind` is the spec's mode (`mount`, `rest` or `none`), never read from the helper. The guard has nothing to watch for such a run: its verdict is `not_applicable`, it stays in the guard's rerun, it is counted apart from `clean`, and the other guards still judge it. **Only that exact statement makes a run not applicable.** A record with no mount-guard snapshot at all (a study that listed the guard on its mount runs alone) is `not_applicable` when the campaign's own fingerprint maps the run's level of the data-path factor (`StudyConfig.data_path_factor`, `[study] data_path_factor`, `data_path` by default; see the study's `[study]` table) to `rest` or `none` (`expcore.mountguard.campaign.recorded_data_paths`, from the `data_paths` the platform records under `platform`; every snapshot of a batch must record the same, and `analyse_study` passes it, with the factor's name, to `judge(record, condition, data_paths, factor)` and `mount_guard_analysis(..., data_paths, factor)`; both the route by a record's own statement, whose data path is checked against the campaign's, and the route by the campaign read the level by that one name), and `unknown` (`not_recorded`) for a level the campaign maps to `mount` or does not map, for a run without the factor, and for a campaign that records none; a statement that names another data path than the campaign records for the run's level is `unknown` (`data_path_mismatch`); a real snapshot is judged as it is. A statement that is not exactly the one above (a data path of `mount`, another name, a version that is not 1, an extra key, `mount` not the boolean `false`) is `unknown` (`not_a_snapshot`). The core never infers the data path from the absence of a snapshot alone, from a missing watcher, or from the name of a level, which only platform.toml maps to a kind; `RunMountGuard.data_path_from` says whether a run's data path came from its `record` or from the `campaign`, and `analysis.json` carries it as `data_path_from` beside `data_path`.
- **The verdict** (`judge(record, condition)`, `verdict_of(snapshot)`): flags `token_access`, `process_access`, `log_unknown` (also `not_recorded`, `instrument_failed`, `not_a_snapshot`) and `isolation_unproven`; `flagged` with an access flag, whatever else; `unknown` with any other flag; `clean` with none; `not_applicable` for the statement of no mount (`RunMountGuard.not_applicable` is its data path, None for a run with a mount; its `log` and `isolation` say `not_applicable`). **A missing or damaged log, or an unproven isolation, is never clean.**
- **The analysis** (`study.pipeline.analyse_study`): once one analysed record carries the namespace, every analysed run is judged, and the preregistered analysis is run again without every run that is not clean (flagged and unknown, each an absent run of reason `excluded`); a run that is `not_applicable` is not left out. `StudyAnalysis.mount_guard` holds both (`expcore.mountguard.sensitivity.MountGuardAnalysis`: `runs`, `sensitivity`, `refused`, `left_out`, `counts()`, which adds `not_applicable` beside `clean`, `flagged` and `unknown`); `analysis.json` gains `mount_guard` (each run's `verdict` and `data_path`, which is None for a run with a mount); the report gains `## Mount isolation (detected, not prevented)` after the egress section (`expcore.report_mountguard`), its rerun's tables titled "without the runs that are not clean"; the terminal summary gains `mount guard:` and `mount sensitivity` lines. A batch in which no record carries it has none of these, byte for byte.
- **Every guard together** (`expcore.guards.guards_analysis(rows, absent, prereg, egress, mount, visibility)`; O60, O76): a batch whose records carry two or more of `egress`, `mount_guard` and `process_visibility` also gets the preregistered analysis again without every run any of those guards leaves out, flagged or unknown to any (a run unknown to one guard and clean to the others is left out). **A guard that does not apply to a run leaves it out for nothing:** a run not applicable to the mount guard is left out only for another guard, and only `clean` and `not_applicable` keep a run, so a state this code does not know leaves it out. Each run is `GuardedRun`: `egress` (`clean`, `flagged`, or `unknown` for an unknown log alone), `mount` (the mount guard's verdict, `not_applicable` included) and `visibility` (the process-visibility verdict), each None for a guard that did not check the batch; `states` those that did, `left_by` the guards that leave the run out, and `by` the one word for them: the guard's name (`egress`, `mount`, `visibility`), `both` when two guards checked the batch and both leave the run out, otherwise the names joined by `+` (`egress+visibility`), or None. `StudyAnalysis.guards` holds it (`GuardsAnalysis`: `runs`, `sensitivity`, `refused`, `left_out`, `guards`, `counts()`, `by_condition()`, each with `analysed`, `left_out`, one `<guard>_only` for each guard that checked the batch and `both` for a batch of two or `several` for three); `analysis.json` gains `guards` (the guards that checked the batch, each run's `egress`, `mount` and `visibility` and `left_out_by`); the report gains a section named for them, `## Egress and mount isolation together (detected, not prevented)` for those two and `## Egress, mount isolation and process visibility together` for all three, after the process-visibility section (`expcore.report_guards`), its rerun's tables titled "without the runs either guard leaves out" for two guards and "any guard" for three; the terminal gains `both guards:` (`all guards:`) and `both-guards sensitivity` (`all-guards sensitivity`) lines, after every single guard's own. A batch checked by one guard has none: that guard's rerun is the combined one. A batch checked by egress and the mount guard alone has the section, keys and lines it had before the process-visibility check joined, with `guards` and `visibility` added to `analysis.json`.
- **Limits.** Detection, not prevention. inotify names no process, so every event in the window counts as the agent's, and it never sees a read of memory the file was mapped into. A tracer is sampled, so one that attaches and leaves between two samples is missed. oneclient's environment (which holds the token) and memory are covered only by the proof that no cell can see its process, never watched. A cell that starts and ends between two polls is not proved itself: the run's proof rests on the cells caught (the start's canary cell holds on for a second) and on every cell's identical command line. A process of the uid that is not a cell (an ssh login into the node, a job step) is outside every check: **no interactive login and no other job step of the account may run on a sandbox node while runs are live** (the study guide's rule, for egress too). oneclient and the watcher carry the run's tag, so the reaper of a driver killed outright kills them as it kills the gate and the collector, after removing the token's file; it unmounts the FUSE mount a killed oneclient leaves (`fusermount3 -u`, then `-uz`), and keeps the run for a later reap when the mount stays.

### Processes hidden from the watch: the `process_visibility` instrument

`expcore.visibility` and the Apptainer backend's watch (O56). On an HPC batch node the agent's memory limit is a watch of the harness's over the cell's processes in `/proc`, which hides a process of the agent's that makes itself non-dumpable. The watch checks from outside (see "The Apptainer backend on an HPC batch node"), and the core reads what it found.

```python
from expcore.visibility import ProcessVisibility

instruments = [..., ProcessVisibility(sandbox)]  # sandbox.process_visibility() -> Mapping | None
```

- **The snapshot** (version 1, under `instruments["process_visibility"]`) has these keys:
  - `memory`: `within`, `over` or `unknown`;
  - `unknown`: the reasons;
  - `hidden_process`: the hidden processes found;
  - `hidden_memory`: the cells killed for hidden memory;
  - `figures`: `margin_bytes`, `start_unexplained_bytes`, `unexplained_peak_bytes`, `cgroup_anon_bytes` and `visible_anon_bytes`, each bytes or None.

  An unknown reading has a reason, and a reading `within` has no reason and nothing hidden. No reading is unknown (`not_watched`), and so is one this code does not read (`not_a_reading`). The record's schema is unchanged (fixture `09-process-visibility.json`).
- **The verdict** (`judge(record, condition)`, `verdict_of(snapshot)`) has three flags:
  - `hidden_process` and `hidden_memory` (a cell the watch killed for hidden memory, whatever its kill reached) make a run `flagged`, whatever else it has: an agent's code that makes itself invisible to the harness is a finding;
  - `memory_unknown` (also `not_recorded`, `instrument_failed` and `not_a_snapshot`) makes it `unknown`;
  - with no flag it is `clean`.

  **A run whose memory reading is unknown is never clean, and its limit condition is unknown, never within its limit.**
- **The analysis** (`study.pipeline.analyse_study`). Once one analysed record carries the namespace, every analysed run is judged, and the preregistered analysis runs again without every run that is not clean (each an absent run of reason `excluded`). It lands in five places:
  - `StudyAnalysis.visibility` (`expcore.visibility.sensitivity.VisibilityAnalysis`: `runs`, `sensitivity`, `refused`, `left_out`, `counts()` with `memory_within`, `memory_over` and `memory_unknown_reading` beside the verdicts and flags);
  - `analysis.json`, under `process_visibility`;
  - the report, as `## Process visibility (detected, not prevented)` after the egress and mount sections and before the guards-together section (`expcore.report_guards`) that follows every single guard's own (`expcore.report_visibility`), whose runs table gives each run's memory reading as its limit condition;
  - its rerun's tables, titled "without the runs that hid from the watch or whose memory is unknown";
  - the terminal, as `process visibility:` and `visibility sensitivity` lines.

  A batch in which no record carries it has none of these, byte for byte. Its verdicts also enter the rerun of every guard together (O76, above).
- **Limits.** Detection, not prevention: hidden memory under the margin, a hidden process's shared memory, pages a visible process shares with its forked children (counted once per process in the visible sum), and a process forked twice under the cell's hidden init (for the `hidden_process` signal) are missed; the step's limit bounds them, and every hidden process ends with its cell.

### Refusing an attempt: the first-turn hook and the post-run check

A manipulation can be defined on the agent's clock: at the agent's first turn, the arriving files are in their declared start state. An instrument that measures it needs a call at that moment to read the state, and a way to say, after the run, that the manipulation was not realised (the start state was wrong, or the plan failed mid-run), so that the attempt is not scored. Two optional methods give it both (C31). An instrument without them is called as it always was.

```python
class StartState:  # an instrument with both; neither is a member of the `Instrument` protocol
    namespace = "start_state"
    def start(self, identity: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None
    def at_first_turn(self, moment: Mapping[str, Any]) -> None  # moment == {"clock_s": <reading of the run's clock>}
    def finish(self) -> Mapping[str, Any]
    def check(self) -> None  # returns when the manipulation held; raises InfraError(<untreatable kind>, detail) to refuse
```

```python
def first_turn_instruments(instruments: Sequence[Instrument], timer: AgentTimer) -> None
# finish_instruments(record, instruments) -> RunRecord  runs the checks, after the snapshots
```
