- `read()` after `launch`'s refusals (no admission, an undeclared input) and before the run's tokens are minted or its sandbox exists, so before any injection the run's body makes: the run's own outage is then in its window, explained by its own journal lines.
- `window(start, owner=...)` once the body has ended and before the sandbox's stop, with the run's owner token (`liveness.owner()`, taken in the run's own context and kept in memory, never recorded). With a fault injector whose `running` is inside the launch (the documented order, `faults.py`: the restore at `running`'s exit, while the run's sandbox and tool server still run), that is after the restore; with one that injects before the launch and restores after it (the pilot's order), it is before the restore and the target reads `held`.
- Each is inside a `sandbox_lifecycle` wait (infrastructure time, never the agent's), off the event loop. A platform with no watch enters the two waits it always did, reads nothing and records the gap (below).

The window is in the sandbox's metadata, `metadata()["infrastructure_facts"]["federation_window"]`, on both sandbox backends (so every Docker run of a platform holds an `infrastructure_facts`, with this fact at least). **The window is also persisted.** `collect()` writes `metadata.json` before the injector's restore, so the `metadata.json` it wrote holds only the placeholder (`unknown`, "the window closes after the run's evidence is collected: see federation-window.json beside metadata.json"). The sandbox remembers each directory `collect` wrote its evidence into (a study's scratch staging included, as each has a `metadata.json`), and at the launch's exit the launch writes the closed window as **`federation-window.json`** beside the `metadata.json` of each one that still exists, atomically: the same JSON as `metadata()` holds, which is also read once the launch has ended (as `teardown` is). A directory that is gone is skipped and one that cannot be written is logged (`the federation's window could not be written beside the run's evidence (PermissionError)`), never a failure of the run; a run that never collected has the window in its metadata alone. The file travels with the rest of the evidence: `merge` copies a run's directory whole and `out_snapshot` walks every file of `out/`. **The replay check (R04) is the one consumer to settle:** a file of a batch that is neither a record, an outcome marker, the snapshot nor a declared side file fails it as `unexpected`, so a study that collects its evidence into its batch has `federation-window.json` to declare as it has `metadata.json`, and one that collects outside its batch is not affected. A directory that is not in the batch is the study's choice, and the core cannot know it. The shape, plain JSON and the same on Compose and on HPC, keyed by label and holding no service, container, step, node, address, path or secret:

```json
{"version": 1, "verdict": "restarted", "backend": "compose", "ownership": "run",
 "window": {"start": "2026-11-17T04:00:00Z", "end": "2026-11-17T04:09:12Z"},
 "services": {"zone": {"status": "stable", "start": "running", "end": "running", "restarts": [], "held": null,
                        "accounting_wait_s_used": {"start": null, "end": null}},
              "providers[1]": {"status": "restarted", "start": "running", "end": "running", "held": null,
                              "accounting_wait_s_used": {"start": null, "end": null},
                              "restarts": [{"label": "outage", "explained": true, "reason": "1 restart(s); the journal holds 1 injection(s) of the service in the window, this run's own"}]}},
 "findings": [], "unknown": [],
 "journal": {"declared": true, "lines_start": 3, "lines_end": 7, "read": true}, "restore": null}
```

`verdict` is the watch's (`finding`, `unknown`, `held`, `restarted` or `stable`), `ownership` is `run`, `unattributed` or `not_checked`, `held` is the service's `held` entry or null, `restore` points at the injector's `restore_verified` and is null unless a service is held, `restarts[].label` is `outage`, `heal` or `finding`, and `accounting_wait_s_used` is the seconds an HPC read waited at the window's `start` and `end` for Slurm's accounting to show a stopped step (null on Compose): it tells how often, and for how long, the accounting trailed a stop. A read that cannot be made is recorded, never raised: the verdict is `unknown` and `unknown` holds the reason, with the exception's type and never its message (`the window's start could not be read (RuntimeError)`), and a window whose start was not read is never `stable`. **A platform given no watch records `unknown` with `reason` "no federation watch configured"** (`{"version": 1, "verdict": "unknown", "reason": ..., "unknown": [...], ...}`, the shape of a window with no service), so a post-check sees the gap and never an absent key, in the metadata and so in the `metadata.json` collect writes; no file is written. A run cancelled during its body, or during the closing read, records `unknown` too ("the run was cancelled before its window was read"; a cancelled body awaits no read) and is still stopped, the cancellation raised. The run goes on in every case, and the body's own error stands over a window that failed: the study's post-check decides what a verdict means for the run.

**Both executor orders are judged.** An executor whose `running` is inside the launch injects after the read and restores before the window: the target restarted, and the window reads `restarted` with the restart labelled `outage` (or `heal`), explained by the run's own injection. An executor that injects before the launch and restores after it (`prepared`, `inject`, the launch, the restore) has the target down at both ends: the window reads `held` for the target and `stable` for the rest, and the restore is the study's to read from the injector's record. Either way the injection must be the run's own (`owner`): another attempt's, or an ownerless line, is a finding.

## The platform layer's files in and out

`expcore_onedata.provision.Provisioner` puts a study's data into its space and reads it back; every write and every range read goes through `expcore_onedata.client.OnezoneClient`'s mutation gate, bound to the provisioner's space (`space`): the target file's space is found by its parent links and its live name read right before each request, and a target in any other space is refused unsent (O09). `Digest`, `Upload`, `Slice`, `tar_members` and `ArchiveRefused` are in `expcore_onedata.streamed`.

```python
class Provisioner:
    def put_file(self, path: str, data: bytes, *, content_type: str = "application/octet-stream") -> str
    def upload(self, path: str, source: Path | str | Slice, *, content_type: str = "application/octet-stream",
               stop: int | None = None, listings: Listings | None = None) -> Upload   # one operation's cache (O67)
    def preallocate(self, path: str, size: int, *, content_type: str = "application/octet-stream") -> str
    def replicate_file(self, file_id: str, size: int, to_provider: str) -> dict   # `replicate`, by id
    def write_range(self, file_id: str, source: Path | str | Slice, start: int, stop: int, *,
                    content_type: str = "application/octet-stream") -> Digest
    def read_file(self, file_id: str, *, provider: str | None = None, dest: Path | str | None = None,
                  size: int | None = None, deadline_s: float | None = None,
                  answers: Callable[[ReadOutcome], None] | None = None, stop: int | None = None) -> Digest
    def fetch(self, provider: str, file_id: str, offset: int, length: int, *,
              answers: Callable[[ReadOutcome], None] | None = None) -> bytes   # a range read, gated (O02)
    def children(self, directory_id: str, provider: str | None = None, *, listings: Listings | None = None,
                 retry: bool | ReadRetry = True) -> dict[str, Entry]   # a policy: a reset reading's (O46)
    retry: ReadRetry            # property: the policy of this provisioner's plumbing reads
    reads_retried: int          # property: the retries made under it and the policies made from it
    def reading_retry(self) -> ReadRetry      # a new policy for one reading of a reset's per-file reads (O46, O51)
    def reading_slot(self, provider: str, reads: ReadRetry) -> ContextManager[None]  # one of the provider's slots
    def blocks(self, file_id: str, provider: str, *, reader: str | None = None,
               retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT,
               deadline_s: float | None = None) -> list[Range]   # one attempt unless the caller is plumbing

class OnezoneClient:
    def __init__(self, config: PlatformConfig, *, http: httpx.Client | None = None, admin_token: str | None = None,
                 environ: Mapping[str, str] | None = None, timeout_s: float = 15.0, kept_links: int = 50_000,
                 collect_after_bytes: int | None = 128 * 1024 * 1024)   # the fallback collection (O47); None: never
    def get(self, path: str, *, provider: str | None = None, token: str | None = None,
            params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, timeout_s: float | None = None,
            retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT, deadline_s: float | None = None) -> Any
    def mutate(self, method: str, path: str, *, provider: str | None = None, json: Any = None,
               params: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None, content: bytes | None = None, timeout_s: float | None = None,
               token: str | None = None, space: Space | None = None, content_type: str | None = None,
               gate_retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT,
               fresh: bool = False) -> Any   # gate_retry: the gate's own reads, never the write; fresh: a new connection
    def read_content(self, path: str, *, provider: str, size: int, chunk_bytes: int, deadline_s: float,
                     sink: Callable[[bytes], None] | None = None, timeout_s: float | None = None,
                     clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic, space: Space | None = None,
                     gate_retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT,
                     answers: Callable[[ReadOutcome], None] | None = None, stop: int | None = None) -> Digest
    def read_range(self, path: str, *, provider: str, offset: int, length: int, timeout_s: float | None = None,
                   space: Space | None = None, gate_retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT,
                   answers: Callable[[ReadOutcome], None] | None = None) -> bytes
    def space_name(self, space_id: str, *, retry: ReadRetry = ONE_ATTEMPT, deadline_s: float | None = None) -> str

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Digest:      # size: int, sha256: str (hex)
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Upload:      # file_id: str, digest: Digest, action: "created" | "skipped" | "resumed" | "restarted", sent_bytes: int
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReadOutcome: # status: int, content_range: str | None, body_bytes: int; to_json() (O37)
```

`path` is relative to the space root; the provisioner works on its space's data provider (`holder` of its `[[spaces]]` entry, or `[placement] holder`).

- **`put_file`** takes the content in memory, refuses a file already at the path, and verifies the size read back. It is for small files, a day-0 scratch file for one.
- **`upload`** never holds the file: it reads and sends it `[placement] chunk_bytes` at a time (16 MiB, 16777216, by default), each chunk a PUT at its offset. A target that already holds the same size and SHA-256 is skipped with nothing sent, whoever created it, and stays out of the change log. One this provisioner created (the change log says so) that holds a strict prefix is resumed from where it ends by offset writes; any other it created that is no longer than the local file is restarted, written again from its first byte with every write at its offset, and one longer than the local file is refused with nothing written, since only a write without an offset could shorten it (O44). A differing file it did not create is refused before anything is written (O09).
- **An ingest's listings cache, and the ingest that holds the space alone** (O67). `upload(..., listings=)` takes one operation's cache of directory listings (`Listings`, keyed by provider and directory id): each directory is listed once, every entry made through it is added, and an entry it holds is read again by its id (`name`, `type`, `size`, `parent_id`) before a skip, a resume or a restart is decided, a directory on the way before it is descended into; one gone, renamed or moved drops the directory's listing, which is read afresh. A name the cache does not hold is taken as absent, so a cache is right only for the space's sole writer. `expcore_onedata.bulkingest`: `ingest_hold(config, *, label, timeout_s=None)` is a context manager holding the federation's lock exclusively with purpose `INGEST` and target `label` (`IngestRefused` on a platform without `[faults]`; `FederationBusy` when the wait runs out; refused while an injection is unsettled), and `ingest_files(provisioner, files, *, root="", on_file=None) -> IngestReport` refuses (`IngestRefused`, nothing sent) unless the context holds that hold for the provisioner's own federation and the manifest (`ManifestFile(path, source, size, sha256)`, paths relative to `root`) is a tree; checks each source's size and SHA-256 before its upload and each upload's digest after it; uploads through one cache; and ends with `check_listing`, a fresh listing of `root`, every level, which raises `IngestMismatch` naming each file missing, not regular or of another size, each directory on the way that is not one, and each entry the manifest does not hold. With no `root` the manifest is the whole space. `IngestReport.files` holds, per file, `index`, `path`, `size`, `action`, `sent_bytes`, `check_s` and `upload_s`; `listing` the counts found and its seconds.
- **An upload from a member of a tar** (O25). `Slice(path, offset, size)` is `size` bytes of a local file from its byte `offset`, read as a file of its own: its offsets are its own (byte 0 is the file's byte `offset`), no read reaches before `offset` or past `offset + size` whatever the `stop`, and `offset` and `size` are whole numbers of 0 or more. `upload` and `write_range` take one wherever they take a local path, with the slice's size as the local file's; a resume or a restart compares the target with the slice's own bytes, and a slice whose file does not hold its whole range is refused (`ProvisionError`) before anything is asked. `tar_members(archive) -> dict[str, Slice]` reads an uncompressed tar's headers (ustar, pax or GNU) and gives each member, by name and in the archive's order, as the slice holding its bytes. It raises `ArchiveRefused` (a `ValueError`), naming why and the member, for a compressed archive or a file that is no tar, and for a sparse member, a link, any other member that is not a regular file (a directory too), a name held twice, a name `rangemap.safe_path` refuses (`/` first, an empty, `.` or `..` part), a pax size that is no plain count and a member whose bytes reach past the archive's end; and for an archive with bytes up to where its reading stopped that no member returned holds (a global pax header is read past), or that does not end in its two zero end blocks and zeros after them there, since `tarfile` ends its reading quietly at a damaged later header. The caller checks each member against its manifest (a hash over `chunks(slice, n)`) and maps the names to paths in its space.
- **No write of content truncates** (O44). A PUT of a file's content without the `offset` query truncates the file to 0 before it writes (op-worker f118ed9, file_content_rest_handler.erl:224-228), which would drop every written block of a preallocated file or a baseline file, so `OnezoneClient.mutate` refuses a PUT to a path ending in `/content` whose `offset` is not a whole number of 0 or more, before anything is read or sent (`TruncatingWrite`, an `OutOfScope`), and every caller in the layer names its offset (the provisioner's writes, the link check's probe, which a longer probe file gives way to the next generation for).
- **`preallocate(path, size)`** makes a new file at `size` bytes with no byte written, in a directory that exists (an existing entry at the path, or a missing directory, is refused before anything is written): it creates the file, logs it, and extends it by an empty write at offset `size`, which on 25.0 sets the size and writes no block (measured on 10 Nov, `deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-10-federation-facts.md`), and checks the size read back. **`write_range(file_id, source, start, stop)`** writes bytes `start` to `stop` of a local file into the same range of a file this provisioner made (one the change log does not hold is refused, O09), chunk by chunk at their offsets, and returns the digest of the bytes sent; a write inside the file's size leaves the size as it was. **`read_file(..., stop=N)`** reads and hashes only the first N bytes of a file of `size` bytes, every answer still checked against that size, so the prefix of a file at its final size is read back without reading its gap (`OnezoneClient.read_content(..., stop=)` likewise). The upload succeeds only when the size and the SHA-256 read back equal those of the bytes it read and sent; otherwise `ProvisionError`. Skipping or resuming reads the target back first, and verifying reads it again, so expect the bytes to cross the network about twice (O25).
- **Every write of content carries `content_type`**, a plain `type/subtype`, `application/octet-stream` (`client.UPLOAD_CONTENT_TYPE`) unless named: Onedata 25.0 answers 415 to a write without one. Only `application/octet-stream` is confirmed on 25.0, and the fake federation refuses any other type.
- **`read_file`** reads a file through `provider` (the data provider unless named; another provider is first waited on until it shows the file, `await_visible`) by ranges of `chunk_bytes`, each gated as a range read bound to the space (a read through a provider that lacks the bytes fetches them), streamed and hashed as it comes, and writes it to `dest` when one is given: to `<dest>.part`, moved into place only when the read is whole and removed when it fails. It is bounded in bytes by `size`, or by the size the file's attributes give, which every answer must match: each must be a 206 for exactly the range asked for, of a file of that size, with exactly its bytes. It is bounded in time by `deadline_s` (`[placement] transfer_timeout_s` unless given), checked before every range and after every piece, with no wait for the platform longer than `request_timeout_s` or what remains of the deadline. `OnezoneClient.read_content` is the same read for a caller with its own `sink`.
- **What the platform answered to a read is reported, not only the bytes** (O37, `streamed.ReadOutcome`). `read_range`, `read_content`, `Provisioner.fetch` and `Provisioner.read_file` take `answers`, a callable that is called once for every answer the read accepted (one for a range read, one per range of a streamed read, in order) with a `ReadOutcome`: `status` (the HTTP status the platform sent, always 206 for an accepted answer), `content_range` (the `Content-Range` header as it was sent, for example `bytes 10-14/100`), and `body_bytes` (the bytes of the body, which is less than the length asked for only when the range was clipped at the file's end, and then `content_range` shows the shorter range). A study that wants to tell a full answer from a clipped one, or a 200 from a 206, reads these and never infers them from the request. An answer that is not accepted reports nothing and raises `PlatformError`: a 200 (the whole file, whatever it holds and whatever `Content-Range` it carries), a 206 with no `Content-Range` or one that is not `bytes first-last/total` of a file of known size, a range that starts anywhere but where it was asked, ends before it starts (an empty answer at the end of the file included) or past the end of the file it names, or whose body does not hold exactly the bytes the header says; a body short of the length asked for is accepted only when the range ends at the end of the file. A failed streamed read has reported the ranges that came whole before it, and no more. The day-0 `provider_outage` check records the REST read's answers in its facts as `answers` (`{"statuses": {"206": n}, "body_bytes": n}`, empty counts when nothing was accepted).
- **`replicate(path, to_provider)` and `replicate_file(file_id, size, to_provider)` return once the target's own block map covers the file on consecutive polls, and schedule again a replication that completed short** (O01). On the 25.0 development build the target runs a replication from its own dbsync copy of the other providers' file locations, while `GET /data/{id}/distribution` through any provider gathers each provider's own map, so a replication scheduled right after a write can fetch only what that copy shows and report `completed`, with nothing in the REST API showing the lag (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-14-federation-facts-replication-lag.md`). While the target's map is short `REPLICATION_CHECK_S` (10 s) after a job ended, the replication is scheduled again after the next of `REPLICATION_RETRY_PAUSES_S` (5, 10, 15, 15 s), at most `REPLICATION_ATTEMPTS` (5) tries. One deadline, the first job's end plus `settle_timeout_s`, bounds everything after that end: a later job's wait is cut to what is left once it is scheduled (a job still running at the deadline is `WaitTimeout`, a job that may still change the replica, as any job that outlives its wait), each check is cut likewise, the last waits out what is left, and no job is scheduled once a check and its pause leave no time; then `NotPlaced` refuses it, naming the tries. As in every bounded poll of the layer, a read begun before the deadline may end after it. A later try that must move more than the time left allows fails closed: raise `settle_timeout_s` for large files. The record is `{"transfer_states": {state: count over every try}, "tries": n, "transfer_ids": [every job's id, in order], "converged_s": seconds from the first job's end until the map covered the file, "placement": {...}, "seconds": ...}`; each job's id is also in the change log (`transfers()`). Each try leaves a transfer record, which never expires once it has ended.
- **A read that timed out is tried again, boundedly and in the log** (O29, `expcore_onedata.readretry`). On 25.0 a read on a provider has hung past its timeout and then answered in a hundredth of a second on a new connection. `OnezoneClient.get` takes `retry`, the call site's choice, which every call site in the layer names: a `ReadRetry` (three attempts, pauses of 2 s and 5 s, a clock, a sleep and a count of the retries made under it; `ReadRetry()` is the plumbing's policy) or `ONE_ATTEMPT`, the default. Only a GET is retried, and only when it timed out (an `httpx` read, connect or pool timeout); the pause and the retry are on the policy's clock and sleep, so a test's ticker moves with them. Never retried: a PUT, POST (the reading POSTs of `query` included), PATCH or DELETE, an answer of any error or 3xx status, a connection error (a provider that is down must look down; only a reset's per-file reads, below, try one again), a write timeout, and a streamed or range read; `get`, the dated read `get_dated` (the reset's history reads its listing's and Onezone's `Date` headers under the reset's policy, the probes' transfer log under `ONE_ATTEMPT`) and the reads that find a target's space (`space_name`, `file_space_id`, `file_space_name`) take `retry`, and `mutate`, `read_range` and `read_content` take `gate_retry`, the policy of their gate's reads alone, so no write, no range read and no streamed read can be handed a policy for itself. Each retry runs on a connection of its own, never one of the pool's, which may be as stale as the one that stalled: a client that built its own HTTP client sends the retry through a one-shot client of the same settings, which is closed when the answer is in, and the connection that stalled is closed by httpcore and dropped from the pool. `deadline_s` bounds the whole call: no retry starts unless its pause and a second more fit in what remains, and a retry's timeout is cut to what remains; `read_file`'s size read and the waits' reads are bounded by their own deadlines and bounds. Each timed-out attempt is logged (logger `expcore_onedata.readretry`) with the seconds it took, the attempt, and how its connection began, `new`, `reused` or `unknown`, and for a `reused` one how many requests it had served before (read from the pool when one connection is active, else "not seen"), and so is the outcome of the attempt after a retry; a line names the provider by its position and holds no path, id or token. The provisioner owns the policy of its plumbing reads (`Provisioner.retry`, `reads_retried`) and the reset and the ingest that work through it use it, so their records say how many reads were retried. **Retried:** the provisioner's listings (`children`, `walk`, `resolve`, `upload`, `make_dir`), its size and attribute reads, `read_file`'s size read and the visibility wait (`await_visible`), the reset's reads (summaries, transfer listings, records and waits, the occupancy, a space's root), the ingest's attribute reads and visibility waits, the root a day-0 mutating check writes under, the setup of the day-0 outage check (its provisioner's helpers and its proof of who holds what), **the mutation gate's reads** and **the polls whose failure ends an operation**, below, and the placement's block-map reads outside a poll. **One attempt:** the health gate's reads, the placement probes' reads (a probe's listing, which `resolve(wait=False)` makes one attempt, and the block map, `Provisioner.blocks`, which is one attempt unless its caller names a policy) with their own counted retry of a transport failure, the transfer log, day-0's reading checks and the outage check's cells (each read and listing during the outage and in its all-up baseline, and the target's direct answer), the pre-campaign content check, which passes the gate too, with one attempt, and whatever a poll that counts a failed read as a poll that did not match reads (the block-map polls of a placement and an eviction, a teardown's absence check, the reset's removal check), since the poll is the retry. `tests/onedata/test_read_retry_sites.py` lists every call site with its choice, the client's own calls and the provisioner's retrying helpers included, and fails on a call it cannot classify, so none changes without that table changing. The scanner takes the client's gated and space reads (`mutate`, `read_range`, `read_content`, `space_name`, `file_space_id`, `file_space_name`) on any receiver, and the dated read on a receiver named for a client, by their `gate_retry` or `retry` keyword, so a measuring module cannot hide a policy behind another name for the client; the layer's functions that retry (`place_ranges`, `evict_ids`, `evict_tree`) count as helpers in their retrying form, and a method got by `getattr` is the call it stands for.
- **A reset's per-file reads also retry a connection failure, never an answer** (O29, O46, O51). A live reset read 8,290 block maps at one provider and then one `GET` failed to connect (`httpx.ConnectError`): the map was unknown and the reset failed closed, 1,238 s lost. Those reads are plumbing, not a measurement of the provider, so each reading goes under a policy made for it, `Provisioner.reading_retry()`: `ReadRetry(TRANSPORT_ATTEMPTS, TRANSPORT_PAUSES_S, transport=True, owner=Provisioner.retry, cap=provider_concurrency)`, four attempts with pauses of 2, 5 and 15 s, which also tries again a GET whose connection could not be made, was reset or was closed before its answer (`client.RETRIED_TRANSPORT`: `httpx` connect, read and write errors, a remote protocol error), each retry on a fresh connection; its retries count in `reads_retried`. The readings: the occupancy (the occupancy stage's, the quiet stage's, the shortcut's narrowed reads and `plan_scan`'s, its counter included, the block maps within what is left of `occupancy.READ_BOUND_S`; `holdings(retry=)` is the reading's policy for every read, a new one when none is named), the QoS scans (the space root, the recursive listing's pages, `summaries` mode's directory listings, `Provisioner.children(retry=<policy>)`, the summaries, the requirements' records), the settle's polls and the quiet stage's maps at T. **Each stage's own transfer reads** go under a policy per stage (`SpaceReset.stage_reads()`, which `resetspaces` calls as each space begins a stage): the transfer listings, the job records, the waits for its jobs (the eviction stage's too, `evict_ids`/`evict_tree(reads=)`, the provisioner's policy by default) and Onezone's clock; each stage that made any records their tally as `transfer_read_retry`. **The gate, the space's name, the eviction's listings and the root** go under the policy of the stage they are made in as well (O09, O46): the mutation gate's reads before every cancel, eviction and requirement's deletion (`Provisioner.mutate(gate_retry=)`, `schedule_transfer(gate_retry=)`), the space's name (`verify_space(retry=)`; the driver's, before any stage, under a policy of its own whose tally is the space record's `verify_read_retry`), the eviction stage's path listings (`resolve_many(retry=)`, `walk(retry=)`), the visibility wait before each eviction (`await_visible(retry=)`: the file's size on the data provider and the wait's reads at the evicting provider, two per file not yet seen there) and the space root's first, kept read (`Provisioner.root(retry=)`, which an instrument's `wait=False` refuses, as `resolve(wait=False, retry=)` does), so the stage's tally counts them too: the qos stage's `read_retry`, the transfers and eviction stages' `transfer_read_retry`, the occupancy's `read_retry`. The listings, the visibility reads and the root hold one of their provider's slots through their retries (`consistency.wait_visible(slot=)`, the wait's pauses outside it); the gate's reads, made inside the mutation's call, hold none. Only the reset passes these policies; every other caller keeps the provisioner's. **A mutation is sent once:** a cancel, an eviction or a requirement's deletion that went past the gate and got no answer back (`client.sent_unanswered`: a transport failure, never a `NotSent`) may or may not have been applied, so it is never sent again, and a later attempt of the eviction schedules none of its files; the job's record (the cancel's wait), the block maps once no transfer is listed running (the eviction's `quiet` and verification; `evict_ids` and `evict_tree` judge a lost eviction so only when given a `quiet` check, and raise it otherwise, as before, and the visibility wait before each eviction is read apart, so a read that fails there is a failed read, never a lost eviction) or the deletion barrier decide, each failing closed at its bound, and the stage's record (an eviction's per provider) counts them as `unanswered` when there was one. **No read a reset makes is left on timeouts only;** the one-attempt polls (the requirements' fulfilment and deletion checks, the eviction's block maps) take a failed read for no match, so one `ConnectError` there fails nothing. **An answer decides at once:** a status (a 404 and a POSIX absence count 0), any other error answer, an unreadable map and a provider's failed storage entry are never asked again, and a failure raised after an error status had arrived (a body cut short: the client marks it, `readretry.ANSWERED`) is final too, so no retry can turn an answer into a pass; a read still failing after its fourth attempt is unread and its reading fails closed. Every other site keeps timeouts only, and `tests/onedata/test_read_retry_sites.py` lists the reading's sites (`READING`) and where a reading's policy is made (`READING_MADE`), never a measuring module. **The record:** each reading's tally, `ReadRetry.facts()`, is `{"policy": {"attempts", "pauses_s", "transport", "provider_concurrency"}, "reads", "retries", "errors": {type: n}, "failed", "answers": {kind: n}, "providers": {label: {"reads", "retries", "errors", "failed", "answers", "slot_wait_s"}}}` (`errors` are failed attempts by exception type, `failed` the reads they ended, `answers` the answers that were no plain success: an error status, a 404 among them, or `"unreadable"`, never tried again), under `read_retry` in the qos, qos_settle, occupancy and quiet stages (the quiet stage's maps under `maps_read_retry`, each stage's transfer reads under `transfer_read_retry`) and in `Holdings.facts()`, a failed stage's included (`readretry.recorded` hangs it on the error's `facts`). **The bound:** `[placement] provider_concurrency` (16 by default) is the most reads of a reset's readings (block maps and the counter; the QoS scans' root, pages, directory listings, summaries and records; each stage's transfer listings and records, its waits and Onezone's clock excepted) in flight at one provider, every space and planner reading of the process together (`readslots.py`, `Provisioner.reading_slot`): a read holds its slot through its retries and pauses, and takes no second one meanwhile. **A good answer cut short is no answer:** a 200 whose body broke off is asked again, and only the retry's whole answer decides, as a timeout in the body always was. **Connection reuse:** the client's own pool keeps every connection it may open alive (`client.POOL_CONNECTIONS`, 100, for httpx's 5 s), so a reading with more than 20 reads in flight, httpx's default keep-alive bound, reuses its connections instead of closing the extra ones as they go idle and connecting again.
- **The mutation gate's reads are plumbing too** (O29). Every PUT, POST and DELETE, every range read and every range of a streamed read first reads the space of its target: its name from Onezone and, for a file, the parent links up to a known root, the first chunk of a new file's upload reading the new file's parent from the data provider, which nothing has cached. A stall there would end the upload or the reset as surely as one in the read the caller asked for, so `mutate`, `read_range` and `read_content` take `gate_retry`, the policy of the gate's reads and of those alone: the provisioner passes its own (`Provisioner.mutate`, `fetch`, `read_file`, and `verify_space` through `space_name(retry=)`), or a reset's stage policy where the reset names one (above), day-0's write check passes its plumbing policy, and a measuring caller leaves the default, one attempt. The write, the range read and the streamed range are one request whatever the policy. The gate stays closed: each attempt that is answered is read afresh and decides, nothing an earlier attempt or call saw is reused (O09), and after the last failed attempt the target is unread, `ScopeUnread` (a transport failure), and nothing was sent. A streamed read's gate makes several reads, and each is given what remains of its `deadline_s` when that read begins, so the retries and pauses of a gate of several reads stay within the one deadline and never add up to a multiple of it; a read that begins after the deadline passed is made once.
- **The polls whose failure ends the operation retry their reads.** `replicate`, `evict` and the bulk eviction (`evict_ids`, `evict_tree`, and so the reset's eviction stage) wait for their transfers by polling each one's record, and a poll that timed out ended the operation, while the reset's cancel wait and its history read already retried the same `GET /transfers/{id}`. They now go under the provisioner's policy, within the wait's own bound. `place_ranges` reads what the receiver holds of the files before it places anything, and of each file afterwards, outside any poll, so those block-map reads (`blocks(retry=)`, `holds(retry=)`) go under the provisioner's policy as well; the probes' and the polls' reads of the same map keep one attempt.
- **A large body is freed when its exchange is used, never at the next collection** (O47, #259). httpx binds every answer to a stream that points back at it, a reference cycle that holds the answer, its request and both bodies until the cyclic collector runs: eight files of 8 MiB uploaded and read back left 128 MiB alive on the public fake, and a long batch or a re-ingest grew by everything it moved, until a collection came at whatever moment it chose. `OnezoneClient` releases every answer the moment it is read (closed, its stream replaced by an empty one), however the read ends, a body cut short included, and an answer httpx itself fails on inside its send (a redirect whose `Location` it cannot parse, a hook of the HTTP client that raises) is found in the frames that failure passed through, released and counted when it answers the exchange's own request, on every path that sends or receives a body: its requests (`get`, `get_dated`, `mutate`, `read_range`), the ranges of `read_content` and the head `content_answer` reads. It keeps no answer, request or body; and a transport failure is raised with its handler over, so a `PlatformError` that says `transport` has no `__context__` or `__cause__`: the httpx error that caused it, with its request, the body and the frames it passed through, is not reachable from it. **It follows no redirect,** whatever the HTTP client it was given is set to: httpx follows one inside its send, where one that fails halfway leaves its answers in their cycles, out of reach of the release, and it takes the `X-Auth-Token` header along to another host (it drops `Authorization` alone). **No 3xx answer is a result:** with no redirect followed the 3xx is the answer, and the client took any status below 400 for success, so a bodyless one decoded to `{}`, an empty success, a false pass for a write as much as for a read. Every 3xx, 300 to 399 (a 304 too, since the client sends no validator), is a `PlatformError` with its `status` and `transport` false, on every path: `get`, `get_dated`, `mutate` (the writes, the creations and the deletes), the provisioner's uploads, `read_range`, the ranges of `read_content` and the head of `content_answer`, and the gate's own reads (a 3xx there leaves the target unread, `ScopeUnread`, and nothing is sent). The message names the status and says whether a `Location` came, never its value (no host, no token); the answer is released first, as every answer is, and its body, which may carry a credential or a host, is never read on a streamed path. It is an answer, not a timeout, so the client's read retry never repeats it (it takes a timeout alone, and a 3xx's body is not read, so a body that stalls cannot raise one), and a probe retries only a `transport` failure. **A poll that counts any failed read as a poll that did not match still polls it again:** `converge`, `converge_each` and what is built on them treat a 3xx as they do a 403 or a 500, so it is read again until the wait's own bound and ends as not converged, never as a pass; a wait that waits out only absence (`wait_visible`, `space_root`) raises it at once. What else a caller sees is unchanged. **The fallback** is the client's `collect_after_bytes` (128 MiB, `collectbudget.COLLECT_AFTER_BYTES`, by default; `None` turns it off; anything but a positive whole number is a `ValueError`): once the bodies sent and received since its last collection reach it, a full `gc.collect()` runs at the start of the next exchange, never in the one that spent the budget, whose own request and answer are still alive when it ends. Every body counts, a JSON request's and a failed exchange's, up to the bytes that came before it failed, and an exchange is counted once its answer is released, so a collection another thread starts at that moment finds no cycle of it. It runs at most once per budget of bytes moved, and it is the net under the fix, not the fix: it is there for a cycle that arises some other way. The fakes' request logs held every body as well, which no client could free (`Request.body`, above, is now kept up to 64 KiB, with `size` and `sha256` for any body). What the fix does not reach is the caller's own: a `PlatformError` a study keeps still holds the frames it passed through, so a study keeps its message and fields and never the exception, as the core's own records do; nor does it reach an HTTP client a study injected whose hooks, authentication flow or transport keep a reference of their own to an answer or a request, or a cycle inside httpx or httpcore that no answer the client holds reaches: the fallback collection frees the unreachable cycles among those, never a reference a caller's own hook or transport keeps alive.
- **A listing ends only on its last-page marker** (O26). A children page ends it only with a boolean `isLast` that is true, whatever its `nextPageToken`, and a page of the transfer listing only with `nextPageToken` null, with no `isLast` asked for; a page without the marker is refused (`PlatformError`, or `Unreadable` in the probes), never taken as the whole directory. These are the shapes measured on 25.0 (`deploy/compose-federation/day0/2026-11-12-federation-facts-listing-end.md`): a children page carries a token on the last page too, a full last page of either listing is followed by an empty one, and the page after a last children page answers 400 `posix` `eagain`. `provision.next_page_token(page)` reads a children page for any caller, and the testing module's fakes answer both listings so (`fake_data.LISTING_END_FACTS`).

## The platform layer's configuration file

`platform.toml` is read by `expcore_onedata.config.load_platform`. It validates the whole file before anything runs, names the file and the key in every message and never prints a value, so a mistake in a URL or a credential source cannot leak a federation's names. Every table refuses a key it does not know and lists the ones it does, so a misspelt key never does nothing. The module docstring of `expcore_onedata/config.py` shows the same layout as one commented example; this section is the reference, with what each table needs.

| Table | Needed | Keys | Read more |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[zone]` | required | `name`, `url`, both required. `url` is a scheme, a host and perhaps a port and nothing after: no credentials, path, query or fragment | |
| `[providers.<name>]` | at least one | `url` and `id`, both required in each; no two providers share an id. A reported provider is matched by its `id` (O04) | The loader's messages and a run's records name a provider by its position, `providers[i]`, never by `<name>` (O12); the day-0 report is the one place that names it by that label |
| `[space]` and `[support]`, or `[[spaces]]` | one form, never both | `[space]`: `name`, `id` and `prefixes` (at least one, each once, and `name` starts with one) are required; `import` and `data_manifest` are optional. `[support]`: `providers` (at least one, each declared) and `local` (one of them) are required. A `[[spaces]]` entry has a required `label` and `holder` (its data provider, one of its providers) beside those keys, and an optional `tokens` (`data_path`, `tool_server`; both by default) | [Spaces and reset](#the-platform-layers-spaces-and-reset), [storage import](#the-platform-layers-storage-import), the docstring of `spaces.py` |
| `[credentials.admin]` | required | exactly one of `env` (the name of an environment variable) and `file` (a path, relative to `platform.toml`) | [Credential files](#credential-files-mode-0600), below |
| `[credentials.users]` | required when `[principals]` or `[slots]` names a user | exactly one of `directory_env` (the name of an environment variable that holds the directory) and `directory` (relative to `platform.toml`); `pattern` is optional, `{user}.pass` by default, with one `{user}` and no path | [Credential files](#credential-files-mode-0600), below |
| `[tokens]` | optional | `margin_s` (900, at least 1), `max_excess_s` (600), `mint_headroom_s` (300, not above `max_excess_s`), `max_ttl_s` (none by default), `revocation_settle_s` (330, at least 315) | [Caps on a run's time](study-guide.md#caps-on-a-runs-time) |
| `[principals.data_path]`, `[principals.toolserver]`, `[principals.service]` | optional | `username` and `user_id`, both required in each, plain names of letters, digits and `_.@-`. `data_path` and `toolserver` are never the same user. They are the fixed users for what leases none: the day-0 checks, health and the live tests | `[credentials.users]` must then hold a file for each |
| `[toolserver]` | optional | `command` and at least one `[toolserver.conditions.<name>]` are required; `args` and `start_timeout_s` (120) are optional, and `clone` and `commit` come together or not at all. A condition has `allow` (at least one tool, each once), `rights` (known rights, each once, the baseline among them) and `manifest`, a file that must exist at load | [The frozen manifest](#the-frozen-manifest) |
| `[tls]` | optional | `ca_file` or `insecure_tls`, never both | [The federation's certificate](study-guide.md#the-federations-certificate) |
| `[addresses]` | optional | `file` (required) and `resolver` | [Dialling a declared name](#the-apptainer-backend-on-an-hpc-batch-node) (O73) |
| `[sandbox]` | optional | `backend` (`docker` by default, or `apptainer`); on Docker `work_root`, `[sandbox.images]`, `[sandbox.network]`, `[sandbox.limits]` and `[sandbox.data_paths]` are required, `[sandbox.mount]` with `[sandbox.images.oneclient]` when a data path is a mount, and `content_check_file` when one is REST | [The sandbox](#the-platform-layers-sandbox) |
| `[placement]` | read by `rangemap.load_placement`, which refuses a file without it | `holder` (required, a declared provider) and the bounds of provisioning, placement and the reset (`poll_s` 2, `settle_timeout_s` 300, `consecutive_polls` 2, and the rest) | [Spaces and reset](#the-platform-layers-spaces-and-reset) |
| `[slots.<name>]` | optional | `data_path` and `toolserver`, each with `username` and `user_id`, and `toolserver.rights`, all required | [Lease slots](study-guide.md#lease-slots-on-the-onedata-platform) |
| `[faults]` | optional | `sanctioned`, `journal`, the services and the bounds of the injector and its heal | [Fault injection](study-guide.md#fault-injection-compare-like-isolation-with-like) |
| `[link_check]` | optional | `name`, `id`, `prefixes`, `providers` (all required) and the bounds, with defaults | [The provider links](study-guide.md#the-provider-links-on-the-onedata-platform), the docstring of `linksettings.py` |

A key's default and its range are in the loader that reads it (`_tokens`, `load_placement`, `load_sandbox`, `load_faults`, `load_link_check`), and a table a loader does not need can be left out whole. Paths in the file are relative to the file. The campaign's fingerprint holds the settings that shape a run and nothing that is one federation's own (`PlatformConfig.fingerprint`).

### Credential files (mode 0600)

A credential is never written in `platform.toml`: the file names an environment variable, a file or a directory. A credential file, the admin's and each user's, must be a regular file (a link is not followed) owned by the user running the process, with no group or other permission bit, that is mode 0600. The check is made on the open file that is then read, so it cannot be swapped between the check and the read (`credentials.py`). With secrets required, which is the default of `load_platform` and what the day-0 command does, loading fails when the admin's variable is unset or empty or its file is unreadable, and when a fixed principal or a leased user has no file in the directory; only the presence, type, owner and mode of a user's file are checked at load, never its contents. A credential must hold at least 4 characters (`MIN_SECRET_LEN` of `expcore.records.redact`), so it can be redacted from a record, and it is registered as a secret the moment it is read.

## The platform layer's day-0 command

A day-0 check is a fact about a deployment measured before a study depends on it, written to a dated report (`expcore_onedata.day0`). The target is always named: there is no default configuration, and nothing is read from the environment to find one.

```sh
uv run python -m expcore_onedata.day0 PLATFORM_TOML --out REPORT_DIRECTORY [--only CHECK]... [--outage-bound-s SECONDS] [--tool-condition CONDITION]
uv run python -m expcore_onedata.day0 --list     # the checks, in the order they run, and which change state
```

Both arguments are required to run. `--only` runs the named checks, repeatable, and refuses an unknown name. `--outage-bound-s` (60) is the longest the outage check waits for one answer while a provider is down. `--tool-condition` names the `[toolserver]` condition whose tools the outage check calls (the first that allows the four knowledge tools by default).

**Consent is in the environment, and exactly `1`** (`day0/runner.py`). Without `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1` the checks that change state report unknown and send nothing. The outage check, which takes a provider down, also needs `EXPCORE_LIVE_FAULTS=1` and `sanctioned = true` in `[faults]`, and starts its sandbox and tool server only with `EXPCORE_LIVE_DOCKER=1` and a `[sandbox]`. The other `EXPCORE_LIVE_*` variables belong to the live tests (`tests/live/`, [`deploy/compose-federation/README.md`](../deploy/compose-federation/README.md)), not to this command.

**The report** is `day0-<UTC time>.json` and `.md` in `--out`, each written new and never over an earlier one. Nothing identifying the deployment is in it: every string is scrubbed of the configured URLs, hosts, ids and space names, providers are named by their labels in `platform.toml`, and a space by its position, `spaces[i]`, when several are declared. A check that raises is recorded as failed with its exception's class and no message.

**Exit status:** 0 every check passed; 1 a check failed; 3 nothing failed but something could not be measured (**unknown is not a pass**: it is listed apart in the report); 2 a usage or configuration error, with the file and the key named on stderr.

| Check | Changes state | Needs from `platform.toml` and the environment | Unknown when |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reachability` | no | `[providers]`; an `id` or one read from the space gives the online flag | a provider's connect time or online flag could not be measured; it fails when one does not answer |
| `health` | no | `[principals.service]` and `[credentials]`; the declared support set | no service user is declared, or the content check measured nothing |
| `tokens` | no | `[principals.data_path]`; with `[toolserver]` and `[principals.toolserver]`, each condition's token | no fixed user is declared, or the fixed tool-server user holds other rights than a condition's, which is then not checked |
| `content_type` | no | a `[sandbox]` with a REST data path and its `content_check_file` | there is no REST data path, or the type could not be read: a pass needs a measurement |
| `listing` | no | the declared spaces' support sets | a root lists no entry, so there is nothing to compare; or it is too large to walk page by page |
| `read_only` | yes | `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`; `[principals.data_path]` | no consent, no fixed data-path user, or a write that was never sent |
| `round_trip` | yes | `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`; `[placement]`; a support set with a provider besides the data provider | no consent, no `[placement]`, or a space held on a read-only imported storage |
| `provider_outage` | yes | `EXPCORE_LIVE_MUTATE=1`, `EXPCORE_LIVE_FAULTS=1`, `[faults]` with `sanctioned = true`, `[placement]`, and three different providers in each space; a sandbox and the tool server need `EXPCORE_LIVE_DOCKER=1`, `[sandbox]`, `[slots]`, `[toolserver]` and a condition that allows the four knowledge tools | any of those is missing, or a mount or a tool was not read |

With several spaces (`[[spaces]]`) a check about a space runs in each space it concerns and its verdict is the worst over them, failed before unknown before passed. The checks that change state clean up after themselves in a `finally` and fail when what they made is not shown gone (O11). The Compose federation's own command line, with the live tests beside it, is in [`deploy/compose-federation/README.md`](../deploy/compose-federation/README.md); the outage check is described in the docstring of `day0/outage.py` and in [the study guide](study-guide.md#after-an-outage-the-session-gate-and-the-link-heal).

## Estimator

`expcore.stats.estimators`. A contrast names its estimator in `preregistration.toml` (`estimator = "..."`), with the estimator's parameters in `[estimators.<name>]`. The core registers two, `wilcoxon_cluster_bootstrap` and `ratio` ([below](#a-ratio-contrast)).

```python
class Estimator(Protocol):
    def __call__(self, paired: PairedDifferences, settings: EstimatorSettings) -> EstimatorResult

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PairedDifferences:
    clusters: tuple[str, ...]  # the rows: the clusters with at least one paired cell, sorted
    models: tuple[str, ...]    # the columns: the models with at least one paired cell, sorted
    matrix: np.ndarray         # clusters x models, the mean difference A - B of each cell; NaN where it has no pair
    pooled: bool = False       # each cluster pools its models in the one column POOLED (a ratio contrast's default unit)
    cluster_differences: np.ndarray  # a property: per cluster, the equal-weight mean over its models with a cell

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EstimatorSettings:
    replicates: int            # [bootstrap] replicates
    level: float               # the interval's level: [bootstrap] ci_level, or 1 - 2 alpha for the equivalence test
    seed: tuple[int, ...]      # [bootstrap] seed and a digest of the contrast's name: the only entropy
    params: Mapping[str, Any]  # [estimators.<name>] as preregistered

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class EstimatorResult:
    estimate: float            # the effect A - B; NaN where it cannot be computed, as every float here
    p_value: float             # two-sided
    ci_low: float
    ci_high: float
    n_clusters: int
    per_model: tuple[ModelEstimate, ...] = ()  # ModelEstimate(model, estimate, ci_low, ci_high, n_undefined_replicates)
    counts: Mapping[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)  # the estimator's own counts, such as zero_dropped

def register(name: str, *, check_params=None, p_values=None, form="difference")  # a decorator above the function
def form(name: str) -> str     # the form of contrast an estimator reads: "difference" or "ratio" (FORMS)
```

How pairs become the matrix's cells is in [the study guide's section 5](study-guide.md#how-pairs-become-a-contrasts-input).

Core guarantees:
- The matrix is read-only, and no row or column of it is all NaN; a contrast with no pair at all gets a `PairedDifferences` with no cluster.
- `check_params(params)`, given at registration, is called when the preregistration is loaded, so a bad parameter is refused naming `[estimators.<name>]`; without one, any parameter is refused.
- For a contrast with an `equivalence_margin` the analysis calls the estimator a second time with `level` at 1 - 2 alpha and the same seed. A NaN p-value counts as p = 1 in the Holm family.
- `p_values`, given at registration, is used to simulate the minimum detectable effect with the contrast's own parameters (S06, S09). Without it the contrast's minimum detectable effect is NaN (`null` in `analysis.json`).
- A row of the matrix is a cluster, the independent unit, and a column a model. A cluster's value is the equal-weight mean of its models' cells (`cluster_differences`), and the core's estimators test those values and resample rows with all their cells, so neither a cell nor a run is a unit of the test, however many models run a cluster. A contrast of one outcome's cell is the mean of its tasks' means, each the mean of its seeds' differences, so each task weighs the same within a model and each model the same within a cluster.
- An estimator reads one form of contrast, `form` at registration, "difference" unless given. A contrast of one outcome needs an estimator of the difference form, a ratio contrast one of the ratio form; the loader refuses any other pairing, naming the key, and `run_contrast` refuses a spec built past the loader.
- `tost`, given at registration as `tost=Tost(p, interval, p_values)`, is the estimator's two one-sided tests for equivalence within plus or minus a margin (S15), in three forms that must agree: `p(paired, settings, margin)` its p-value, `interval(paired, settings, level)` its dual interval at 1 - 2 x level (inside the margin exactly when p <= level), and `p_values(studies, margin, params)` its p-value of every row of a studies x clusters array, which the feasibility simulation judges each study by. Only an estimator with one can be a member of an `equivalence_family`. `estimators.tost_function(name)` returns it, or None.

**An equivalence family (S15, #140).** `equivalence_family = ["name", ...]` names members of `holm_family` that declare an `equivalence_margin`, each once; it is refused beside `exploratory_only = true`. The Wilcoxon cluster bootstrap's TOST is Schuirmann's two one-sided t-tests of the mean cluster difference, its estimate (`contrasts.tost_t`): with the n cluster differences (after float noise is snapped), their mean m and standard error se = sd / sqrt(n), the p-values of theta > -margin and theta < margin are the t distribution's tails on n - 1 degrees of freedom beyond (m + margin) / se and (m - margin) / se, and the TOST p is the larger. It assumes independent clusters whose differences are roughly normal, which a few dozen clusters make robust. Fewer than two clusters, or no spread between them, give NaN: no evidence, never certainty. Its interval at 1 - 2L is m plus or minus the t quantile at 1 - L times se (`tost_t_interval`), so TOST p <= L exactly when it lies within the margin. Holm over the family's TOST p-values decides (`AnalysisResult.equivalence_holm`): a member is `equivalent` only when Holm rejects its two one-sided nulls at its step's level L, an untestable member, or one with a NaN TOST p, counting as p = 1 and never equivalent. It carries `tost_p`, `tost_p_holm`, `equivalence_level` = 1 - 2L and `equivalence_interval_adjusted`, the t interval at that level; `equivalence_interval` stays the 1 - 2 alpha bootstrap interval, which no longer decides for it. `run_contrast` called alone judges a member's raw TOST p at alpha. A contrast with a margin outside the family keeps the bootstrap interval rule at 1 - 2 alpha, whose percentile interval cannot be wider than its clusters: with one or two clusters it can lie inside a margin that no evidence supports, which is why a confirmatory equivalence claim belongs in a family.

**Feasibility before the campaign.** `analysis.equivalence_feasibility(prereg, rows=None) -> PowerResult` gives, per member of the equivalence family and per SD (each of `[power] sd_grid`, or with pilot rows the contrast's own between-cluster SD), the chance of concluding equivalent at a true difference of 0 and of half the margin: `contrast`, `sd`, `n_clusters` (`target_clusters`), `margin`, `alpha` (alpha over the family size, Holm's first and hardest level, so conservative for all but the member with the smallest p-value), `delta`, `probability` and `reachable` (the chance at 0 meets `target_power`; never for NaN). `power.equivalence_feasibility(sd, n, margin, alpha, n_sim, rng, deltas, p_values=None)` simulates it: each study draws n cluster differences from N(delta, sd^2) and is judged by `p_values`, the member's estimator's own `Tost.p_values` under its preregistered parameters, as the minimum detectable effect is; by default by the two one-sided t-tests written again in the power module (`power.tost_t_p_values`), which imports nothing of the pipeline (S09). It needs a `[power]` section and an equivalence family; a margin outside the family is another rule and is not simulated.

Implementer must guarantee:
- The estimator is a pure function of its arguments: called twice with equal settings, it returns equal results, whatever ran in between. NaN, never a guess, where it cannot compute a value.
- `p_values(studies, params)` returns, for each row of a studies x clusters array of cluster differences, exactly the p-value the estimator reports for that row.
- The name is lower-case letters, digits and underscores, registered once: a second registration of a name is refused.

**Where a study's estimator is registered.** The preregistration is validated when the study is loaded, before its plugin is imported, so a name only the plugin registers is unknown at that point and the contrast is refused (`unknown estimator 'name'; registered: wilcoxon_cluster_bootstrap, ratio`). A study cannot register its estimator through its plugin. It registers it at the top level of a module that `[study] platforms` names, which the loader imports before it validates anything, as it does for a platform's infrastructure kinds. That module must be importable, so it is installed code, not a file of the study directory, which is not on the module search path: a distribution of the study's own, named in `[study] requires`, so it is part of the campaign's environment and the replication package vendors its wheel. Its version speaks for its code, so change the version when the estimator changes. Once registered, the estimator stays registered for the rest of the process.

### A ratio contrast

`expcore.stats.ratio` (S13, #138). A contrast of an outcome per unit of another, such as tokens per correct result, names an estimator of the ratio form and two outcomes in place of `outcome`:

```toml
[contrasts.cost-per-success]
conditions = ["cond-a", "cond-b"]
estimator = "ratio"
numerator = "tokens"
denominator = "success"
zero_denominator = "exclude"   # or "continuity", with continuity_correction = c > 0 (required with it, refused without)
cluster = "group"              # the independent unit, as for every contrast
estimation_unit = ["model", "group"]  # optional: sums per model's cell of a group; ["group"], the default, pools them
equivalence_margin = 0.2231    # optional: a natural-log ratio, here within a factor of 1.25 either way

[contrasts.cost-per-success-adverse]  # each arm its own column: arm A at its upper bound, arm B at its lower
conditions = ["cond-a", "cond-b"]
estimator = "ratio"
numerator_a = "tokens_high"    # optional, as are numerator_b, denominator_a and denominator_b: each defaults to
numerator_b = "tokens_low"     # the shared numerator or denominator
denominator = "success"
zero_denominator = "exclude"
cluster = "group"
estimation_unit = ["model", "group"]
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RatioForm:                # ContrastSpec.ratio, and ContrastResult.ratio (None for a contrast of one outcome)
    numerator: str | None       # the column both arms read; None when the arms read their own
    denominator: str | None
    zero_denominator: str       # "exclude" or "continuity" (ZERO_RULES)
    continuity_correction: float | None = None
    numerator_a: str | None = None    # arm A's own column, None when it reads the shared one; so for the other three
    numerator_b: str | None = None
    denominator_a: str | None = None
    denominator_b: str | None = None
    def columns(self, arm: int) -> tuple[str, str]  # (numerator, denominator) arm 0 (A) or 1 (B) reads
    per_arm: bool               # a property: the arms read different columns for either role
    label: str                  # a property, "numerator/denominator", or "na/da vs nb/db" per arm: the contrast's `outcome`
ROLES = ("numerator", "denominator")
ARM_SUFFIXES = ("a", "b")
OMIT_NONE = "omit_none"         # field metadata: analysis.json leaves the field out while it is None

def log_ratio(arm_a: Totals, arm_b: Totals, form: RatioForm) -> tuple[str | None, float]  # (cause, NaN) or (None, value)
def aggregate(cells: Mapping[str, Sequence[tuple[Totals, Totals]]], form) -> tuple[PairedDifferences, Excluded, Excluded]
def aggregate_by_model(cells: Mapping[tuple[str, str], Sequence[...]], form) -> tuple[PairedDifferences, Excluded, Excluded]
Excluded = dict[str, int]       # the pairs, then the cells, the zero rule excluded, by each of ZERO_CAUSES
ZERO_CAUSES = ("zero_denominator", "zero_numerator")
RATIO_CAUSES = ("numerator_none_at_zero", "zero_denominator", "zero_numerator")
POOLED = "pooled"               # the one column of a ratio contrast's input when its cells pool a cluster's models

# expcore.stats.pairing
ContrastSpec.cluster: str                      # the task attribute that clusters the contrast: the independent unit
ContrastSpec.estimation_unit: tuple[str, ...]  # the cell a value is computed in: ("model", cluster) for a contrast of
                                               # one outcome; (cluster,), the default, or ("model", cluster) for a ratio
ContrastSpec.by_model: bool                    # a property: the estimation unit names the model
Pairing.n_cells: int                           # cells of the estimation unit with a value
Pairing.cells_lost: Mapping[str, int]          # cells seen in either arm and left without a value, by cause
MODEL_KEY = "model"
CELL_CAUSES = ("no_pair",)                     # why a cell has no value; a ratio contrast adds ZERO_CAUSES
# expcore.stats.analysis
ContrastResult.estimation_unit: tuple[str, ...] | None  # the spec's; None only in a result built by hand
ContrastResult.independent_unit: str | None             # the cluster; None only in a result built by hand
ContrastResult.n_cells: int | None
ContrastResult.cells_lost: Mapping[str, int]
```

**Two units.** Every contrast has two. Its `cluster`, one task attribute, is the independent unit: the signed-rank test, the cluster bootstrap and the minimum detectable effect count its values, and `n_clusters` is their number. Its estimation unit is the cell one value is computed in, nested in a cluster. A contrast of one outcome always estimates per model's cell, `("model", cluster)`, and averages a cluster's cells with equal weight per model ([above](#estimator)). A ratio contrast declares its estimation unit, `estimation_unit`, an array (or one string) of the cluster and, beside it, `"model"`, in either order and held as `("model", cluster)`; the two are two estimands:
- the cluster alone, the default (`["group"]`): a cell is a value of the attribute, and its sums run over every task, model and seed of it, so its models are pooled. A model with no correct result in the value still adds its cost there, and the models that spend the most weigh the most in its ratio.
- the model and the cluster (`["model", "group"]`): a cell is one model's part of a value, its sums run over that model's tasks and seeds alone, and the value's log ratio is the mean of its cells' log ratios, each model weighing the same, as a contrast of one outcome averages its models. This is the nested design of a power analysis that estimates per (model, instance) cell and infers over instances: 150 instances and four models are 600 cells and 150 units of the test.
The cells of one value share its response to the treatment and are not independent of one another, so no declaration makes them units of the test, on a confirmatory contrast or an exploratory one: `cluster` is a string, and an array there is refused, one that names the model with that reason and the declaration above (`cluster = "group"` with `estimation_unit = ["model", "group"]`). `estimation_unit` beside an estimator of one outcome is refused with the other ratio keys, and so is any unit but the two (another attribute, a field of the run key, none, or a field written twice). Both runs of a pair share the model, which is part of every pairing key, and the check that each task has one cluster stays on the attribute, so a task run by several models is in one value and in several cells.

**The estimand.** A cell's value is ln((N_A / D_A) / (N_B / D_B)), where N_a and D_a are the sums of the numerator and of the denominator over the cell's runs of arm a that belong to a used pair, every task and seed of the cell together. It is a ratio of sums, not a mean of per-run ratios: a run whose denominator is 0, a failed run, has no ratio of its own and still adds its numerator. A cluster's value is its one cell's, or the equal-weight mean of its models' cells, and theta is the mean over clusters of their values; exp(theta) is a geometric mean over clusters of arm A's ratio over arm B's, so "x2" and "x1/2" are symmetric on this scale. Within a cell the sums weigh runs, not tasks, nor models when the cell pools them (a contrast of one outcome weighs each task and each model alike). The value is computed in exact rational arithmetic up to the logarithm, so arms with one ratio give 0.0, never float noise, and no ratio of sums can overflow.

**The inference.** The input is a `PairedDifferences` with one row per cluster: when a cell is the cluster, in one column, `POOLED`, the input marked `pooled`; when a cell is a model's, one column per model, not marked `pooled` whatever the models are called, each cell's log ratio in its cluster's row and its model's column, NaN where the cell is lost. The `ratio` estimator is the kernel's on that input, as the difference estimator is on differences: a cluster's value is the equal-weight mean over its models with a cell, the estimate the mean of the clusters' values, the p-value the two-sided Wilcoxon signed-rank test of those values (float noise snapped, zeros dropped and counted as `zero_dropped`, all zeros p = 1; it tests their centre of symmetry), and the interval the cluster bootstrap's percentile interval at the contrast's seed, each draw a sample of clusters with all their cells. Its parameters are the Wilcoxon cluster bootstrap's (`[estimators.ratio]`, `snap_decimals` and `method`), and it registers the same p-value function, so the minimum detectable effect is simulated with the test it reports, from the SD of the clusters' values at their count. Pooled, it has no estimate by model (`per_model` is empty); by cell, `per_model` holds each model's mean log ratio over its own cells with its interval from the same draws of the clusters (S04), an exploratory breakdown with no test of its own. The estimate, both intervals, the margin and the minimum detectable effect are natural-log ratios; the Holm family and the conclusion words take it as any contrast.

**A zero sum.** A cell whose numerator or denominator sums to 0 in either arm has an undefined or infinite log ratio, and the preregistration's `zero_denominator` decides; it has no default. The rule applies to each cell, so with the model in the estimation unit a model with no correct result in one arm of a value loses that cell alone, and the value keeps its other cells:
- `"exclude"`: the cell leaves the contrast; each of its pairs is counted under `zero_denominator` (a denominator sum of 0 in either arm, 0 / 0 included) or else `zero_numerator`, not in `n_pairs`, and the cell under the same cause in `cells_lost`. A cluster is dropped, and counted in `n_clusters_dropped`, only when every cell of it is lost; a contrast left with no cluster is `untestable`.
- `"continuity"`: `continuity_correction` c is added to all four sums of every cell, a zero or not, in each outcome's own units, so every cell stays. It defines a corrected estimand: c moves each cell's log ratio, most where its sums are small, and not always toward 0 (arms of one ratio, 100 / 10 and 10 / 1, give 0.31 with c = 0.5).

**A failed run's missing numerator.** A pair whose run has a None numerator and a denominator of 0 is counted under `numerator_none_at_zero` (after a treatment attempt's None, before any other None, which is `outcome_none`): leaving a failed run's cost out makes the ratio one over successful runs. A member of the Holm family is refused (`analysis.RatioNumeratorRefused`, naming the contrast, the count and the fix) when any pair both arms analysed holds an ordinary run whose numerator is None at a denominator of 0, whatever single cause the pair is counted under (`Pairing.n_unmeasured_failures`), so a preregistered `max_unscored_treatment` that excuses the treatment attempt beside it does not excuse it; an exploratory contrast reports the count of its cause. `TreatmentOutcomesRefused` and `RatioNumeratorRefused` are both `analysis.ContrastRefused`, which `analyze` reports as a refusal. A negative numerator or denominator is refused naming the run and the outcome.

**Two analyses on two columns.** Each ratio contrast names its own `numerator` and `denominator`, so a study that bounds an outcome declares one contrast per bound, for example the estimate on lower-bound token columns and the adverse assignment on upper-bound ones, each with its own name, seed, result and Holm membership. A run whose upper bound is None at a denominator of 0 is counted, or refuses the contrast if confirmatory, only in the contrast that reads that column.

**One analysis on a column per arm.** A contrast may give each arm a column of its own, `numerator_a` and `numerator_b` for arm A's and arm B's numerators, and `denominator_a` and `denominator_b` for their denominators, each defaulting to the shared `numerator` or `denominator`. An adverse sensitivity estimate that puts arm A at its upper-bound tokens and arm B at its lower-bound ones is then a declared contrast of its own beside the main one, with `numerator_a = "tokens_high"` and `numerator_b = "tokens_low"` (or `numerator = "tokens_low"` with `numerator_a = "tokens_high"`, the same contrast). In each cell N_A and D_A are summed from arm A's columns and N_B and D_B from arm B's, ln((sum X_A / sum D_A) / (sum Y_B / sum D_B)), and the rest is as for any ratio contrast. Per-arm keys override the shared one; mixing that leaves a column unread or an arm without one is refused, naming the key:
- the shared key beside both per-arm keys of its role, when neither names it: no arm would read it;
- one per-arm key with no shared key: the other arm has no column;
- an arm whose numerator and denominator would be one column;
- a per-arm key, or any other ratio key, beside an estimator of one outcome.
A per-arm key that names the shared column declares nothing, and its contrast is the one without it, record for record. The form is held one way whichever way it is written: a role whose arms read one column holds it as `numerator` (or `denominator`) with the per-arm fields None, and a role whose arms differ holds both arms' columns with the shared field None. Each run is read in its own arm's columns, found from the run's own condition or level of the varying factor (`pairing._columns`), for its value, a failed run's None numerator and its arm's count alike, so no part of the analysis reads a run in the other arm's column: a run of arm B need not have arm A's column, a run of arm A without its own is refused naming the column, and a failed run whose numerator is None in its own arm's column refuses a confirmatory contrast (`RatioNumeratorRefused`, naming each arm's columns), while a None in the column only the other arm reads leaves the pair used.

**In the outputs.** `analysis.json` gives every contrast `ratio` (the form, or null), its two units, `estimation_unit` (`["model", "group"]` for a contrast of one outcome, `["group"]` by default for a ratio contrast) and `independent_unit` (the cluster), and its cells, `n_cells` and `cells_lost` by cause (`no_pair`, then a ratio contrast's `zero_denominator` and `zero_numerator`, zeros included); a ratio contrast estimated per model's cell its `per_model`, and a ratio contrast's `pairs_missing` the three `RATIO_CAUSES` after the five others, zeros included; `n_pairs_missing` counts the None causes, `numerator_none_at_zero` among them. `report.md` shows a ratio contrast's outcome as `ln(numerator/denominator)`, adds a section "Ratio contrasts" (each one's two outcomes, its estimation unit, its independent unit, its cells used and lost by cause, its rule, and exp of its estimate and interval, then, for a contrast estimated per model's cell, "Ratio contrasts by model (exploratory)" with each model's estimate, ratio and interval) and the three causes as columns of the pairs tables, `-` in the row of a contrast of one outcome, and the terminal summary marks its estimate `(ln ratio)`. A report with no ratio contrast has none of these, byte for byte as before. A contrast whose arms read different columns has them in its `ratio`, `numerator_a` and `numerator_b` (or `denominator_a` and `denominator_b`) with `numerator` (or `denominator`) null, and its `outcome` is `"tokens_high/success vs tokens_low/success"`, arm A's ratio first; the per-arm fields of a form are left out of `analysis.json` while they are None (`OMIT_NONE`), so a contrast that declares none is written exactly as before they existed. The report's Numerator (or Denominator) cell then names each arm's column with its arm, `cond-a: tokens_high; cond-b: tokens_low`, its outcome cell is `ln(tokens_high/success vs tokens_low/success)`, and a note after the ratio notes, only in a report with such a contrast, says each arm reads its own columns.

### A smallest effect of interest

`expcore.stats.smallest` (S16, #81). A contrast may declare the smallest effect that counts, in the form its estimator reads, and the sign it claims:

```toml
[contrasts.a-vs-b]             # a contrast of one outcome
smallest_effect = 0.1          # a positive difference on the outcome's scale, here 10 points of a proportion
direction = "higher"           # optional: "higher" (A above B) or "lower"; left out, the rule holds either way

[contrasts.cost-per-success]   # a ratio contrast
smallest_ratio = 2             # a positive ratio other than 1, a factor of two, compared as ln 2 = 0.6931
direction = "lower"            # optional: A's cost per success at most half of B's
```

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Rule:                       # ContrastSpec.smallest_rule; None when the contrast declares none
    form: str                     # "difference" (smallest_effect) or "ratio" (smallest_ratio)
    declared: float               # as preregistered
    direction: str                # "higher", "lower", or "either" when none is declared
    threshold: float              # a property: the difference, or |ln ratio|, on the scale of the estimate

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SmallestEffect:             # ContrastResult.smallest_effect; None, and absent from analysis.json, without one
    form: str
    declared: float
    threshold: float              # on the estimate's scale
    direction: str
    decision: str                 # one of DECISIONS
    interval: str | None          # one of INTERVAL_RELATIONS; None when the interval is undefined
    detectable: bool | None       # the minimum detectable effect is at most the threshold; None when unknown
    minimum_detectable_ratio: float | None  # a ratio contrast's exp(minimum detectable effect); else None

DECISIONS = ("meets", "meets_either_direction", "opposite_direction", "falls_short", "not_significant", "untestable")
INTERVAL_RELATIONS = ("beyond", "includes", "short_of")
def rule(*, smallest_effect=None, smallest_ratio=None, direction=None, ratio: bool) -> Rule | None  # RuleError(key)
def decide(significant, estimate, threshold, direction, untestable=False) -> str
def interval_relation(low, high, threshold, direction) -> str | None
def detectable(minimum_detectable_effect, threshold) -> bool | None
```
